# FINDINGS.md — the open findings register

Every finding this build track has convicted but not yet closed, with the file that
convicts it. Written 2026-09-08 because the register existed only in agent session
state: `db/checks/` holds the evidence and `db/migrations/` holds the fixes, but
nothing in the repo said which findings were still **open**. A finding that lives
only in a session dies with it.

**Scope and provenance.** Reconstructed from the working task register on 2026-09-08.
Items are listed with the file that convicts them where one exists, and marked
*not yet written up* where the finding is recorded only as a task line — those
entries state the task line verbatim rather than a reconstruction of it.

Closed items are listed too, in one line each, because "what has already been
fixed" is the question a reader asks second.

---

## Open

| # | Finding | Evidence | Fix | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G121** *(from [0326](db/checks/0326_three_fast_chargers_are_held_by_vehicles_that_are_in_bays_and_the_rule_that_would_say_so_has_no_probe.sql) §2–§3)* | **Three of the twin depot's ten DCFC stalls read `status='available'` while `current_vehicle_id` and `reserved_by` still point at a vehicle that is physically in a bay — and no sweeper will ever clear them.** Persistent across ticks (3 at 07:16:15, 07:16:41, 07:17:27). Every other stall type is internally consistent (pointer set iff `occupied`), so this is one unhandled transition — charger→bay — leaking three rows. **Both candidate reconcilers exclude it by explicit design:** `ottoq_release_vacated_spaces` phase (b) CAN clear a pointer but is scoped `stall_type IN ('wash_bay','service_bay')` with the comment *"Never dcfc/l2/staging"*; `ottoq_release_departed_spaces` (0329) reaches non-bay stalls but is **calendar-only**, so it cannot clear a pointer even if it ran — and it does not run (catalog default 0; the single stored row is `run`-scoped to `39c7400a` from 2026-09-15, long purged). | `0326` §2 the three-stall table with pointed-vehicle states; §3 the two-sweeper pincer proved from comment-stripped `prosrc`, and the dial resolving to 0 on the live run. | **Not a sweeper bug — a deliberate design meeting an unhandled transition.** The narrow repair is to clear the charger pointer where the vehicle enters a bay, not to widen a sweep that races the charge-session path that owns that column. | open |
| **G122** *(from [0326](db/checks/0326_three_fast_chargers_are_held_by_vehicles_that_are_in_bays_and_the_rule_that_would_say_so_has_no_probe.sql) §5)* | **`HW.006.physical_presence_verification` — `critical`, `enforcement='block'` — is the rule written to detect G121, and it has never been evaluated once.** Its evaluator reads `stalls.current_vehicle_id`, and on disagreement returns *'system state mismatch: stall does not record this vehicle as present'* with `suggested_action='reconcile_state_or_dispatch_tech_check'` — G121, named, with the repair prescribed. It is declared, its evaluator exists and compiles, and its only action context is `task_completion`, which nothing probes. | `0326` §5; §7(b)'s dry run over the ten in-progress atoms. | **Do not wire it naively.** Dry-run: 2 of 10 would FAIL and **8 would pass on "insufficient context"** because `cabin`/`exterior` atoms have no stall at all (0383: exterior work is performed at the vehicle, `lane_stalls=NULL`). HW.006 assumes every completion happens AT A STALL and most of this engine's work does not. The probe must be scoped to atoms that genuinely occupy one. **BUILT by [0418](db/migrations/0418_the_probe_for_the_one_unprobed_context_the_engine_actually_exercises_built_and_proven_but_not_wired.sql), and that scoping conclusion was REVERSED while building: suppressing the probe when there is no stall also suppresses HW.003, SLA.003, SM.002 and TW.002, none of which need one — it would throw away four working checks to spare one rule an honest abstention, and the abstention is HW.006's own declared behaviour. The discipline went into the accounting instead (`ottoq_assert_task_completion_coverage`). Proven on live atoms: HW.006 fired with `would_block=true` and the exact G121 reason. NOT WIRED — one PERFORM in `twin.ottoq_sim_advance_visit_atoms`' done-branch, held because `rules` is one of the fourteen determinism atoms so the wiring migration must be forces_recert TRUE and resets the canon matrix.** | open — built, proven, unwired |
| **G123** *(from [0326](db/checks/0326_three_fast_chargers_are_held_by_vehicles_that_are_in_bays_and_the_rule_that_would_say_so_has_no_probe.sql) §6, supersedes the arithmetic in G44)* | **The shield's coverage is 34 of 69 declared (code, context) pairs — 49% — not the 80% the code count reads.** Measured: 31 declared action contexts, 9 probed, **22 never probed**, 0 orphan probes; 69 (code, context) pairs, 34 probed. A code scores as "evaluated" if **any one** of its contexts is probed, so a rule declaring five contexts and probed at one counts as fully wired. **`task_completion` alone is declared by five codes** — HW.003 sensor_liveness, HW.006, SLA.003 max_visit_duration, SM.002 task_transition_validity, TW.002 — and **three of the five count as evaluated because they fire at `task_start`: the engine checks sensor liveness before work begins, never after it ends, and scores that covered.** Also: at code level it is now **24 of 30 at nine probe points, six unevaluated, three of them critical** — SM.001/003/006 are no longer unevaluated, being the one code each on the three row-level triggers 0321 found. | `0326` §6, the pair census and the per-context table with the evaluated-elsewhere column. | **Quote 34 of 69 AND 12,478 of 12,871 TOGETHER — never 49% alone.** `ottoq_assert_shield_coverage(run)` already measures a third unit, enacted decision ROWS: on the live run 12,871 enacted, **12,478 shielded on the row (96.9%)**, 393 unshielded of which 305 gated downstream, 61 record a fact and only **27 a real GAP (0.2%)**, one branch (`gate_intake_no_charge`, space/movement ungated). So the declaration surface is 49% wired while the traffic is 96.9% shielded — different questions, both true, and quoting 49% alone is the error `0417` §2 warned about. **The reconciliation is the real finding:** the 22 unprobed contexts are mostly actions this engine never performs (SM.004's seven are human/UI; `oem_acceptance`/`release`/`power_increase` rename transitions already gated at `redeployment`/`charge_session_start`). **`task_completion` is the exception — the engine completes atoms constantly — which is exactly why HW.006's absence is a live defect (G121) and SM.004's is not. The test that separates a harmless declaration gap from a real one is not the count, it is whether the engine performs the action.** 34 of 69 is still the number CLAUDE.md 2.5's *"wiring count, not a protection count"* caveat was reaching for. | open |
| **G124** *(from [0326](db/checks/0326_three_fast_chargers_are_held_by_vehicles_that_are_in_bays_and_the_rule_that_would_say_so_has_no_probe.sql) §6(a))* | **"The unwired rules have evaluator functions that exist and are callable, and no caller" was measuring the search, not the engine.** A static scan of every function body for an evaluator's name returns **zero callers for SM.001, SM.003 and SM.006 too** — and those three fire 947, 924 and 14 times. The shield reads `evaluator_function` out of `ottoq_rules` and invokes it with dynamic SQL via `ottoq_evaluate_rule_core`, so the name appears in no caller's source. **A static caller search can never find a rule's wiring.** | `0326` §6(a): static_callers = 0 for all four codes beside their actual evaluation counts. | Retire the "no caller" frame wherever it appears. The determinant is whether any probe passes a context the rule declares — see G123. | closed — frame retired |
| **G125** *(from [0326](db/checks/0326_three_fast_chargers_are_held_by_vehicles_that_are_in_bays_and_the_rule_that_would_say_so_has_no_probe.sql) §1, ninth instance of the clock-domain class)* | **`ottoq_stall_bookings.during` is a `tstzrange` in SIM time; computed against `now()` a calendar-availability census reports an EMPTY DEPOT.** On the live run the clocks are four hours apart (real 07:16:41, sim 11:25:08). Right (sim) vs wrong (`now()`) calendar-free: dcfc **2** vs 10, l2 **4** vs 30, staging **54** vs 113 — every stall type reads 100% free under the wrong clock, on a run holding 116 vehicles. Proof `during` is sim: `min(lower(during))` equals `min(booked_at_sim)` to the microsecond for `temp_hold`, `charge_l2` and `charge_dcfc`. **I had the wrong figure in hand and was reasoning from it.** | `0326` §1, both columns side by side plus the microsecond-equality proof. | Any availability or capacity query touching `during` must use the sim clock. **CLAUDE.md Part 3 was checked and is NOT affected** — its recorded "L2 read 3 pointer-free against 1 calendar-free" would have read 30 under the wrong clock, which exonerates it. | open — a guard belongs in the availability helpers |
| **G126** *(from [0326](db/checks/0326_three_fast_chargers_are_held_by_vehicles_that_are_in_bays_and_the_rule_that_would_say_so_has_no_probe.sql) §4)* | **The two availability gates fail in opposite directions on the same reading, which is CLAUDE.md Part 3's "neither gate dominates" rule caught mid-act.** At one instant: `dcfc` pointer-free **0 of 10** against calendar-free **2 of 10** (pointer pessimistic), while `service_bay` pointer-free **2 of 2** against calendar-free **0 of 2** (calendar pessimistic). Quoting the pointer makes the bays look free and quoting the calendar makes the chargers look free — in the same query, at the same instant. The empty-but-booked bays are `perimeter_walkaround` `purpose='service'` holds with `current_vehicle_id IS NULL`. | `0326` §4 and §1's per-type gate table. | Availability must stay the intersection of all three gates (Part 3). Whether a walkaround should book a bay at all is 0383's open question, not this one's. | open |
| **G127** *(from [0419](db/migrations/0419_my_own_abstention_counter_counted_two_of_the_four_abstentions_as_real_verdicts.sql) §2)* | **`HW.003.sensor_liveness` fires at task completion and nobody predicted it.** Exercising 0418's probe on live in-progress atoms returned `passed=false`, `would_block=true`, `safety_critical`, reason *"SOC sensor stale: 300 seconds old (threshold 300)"*. HW.003 is evaluated today only at `task_start` and `redeployment`, **so a vehicle whose SOC sensor goes stale DURING the work is never checked** — G123's `task_completion` gap producing a real failure the moment it is looked at, rather than a theoretical one. | `0419` §2, the probe's verdict table over live atoms (writes rolled back). | Comes free with wiring the `task_completion` probe (G122). **The wiring must never act on `would_block`:** HW.003 and HW.006 are both `enforcement='block'`, and blocking a *completion* is incoherent — the work is already done, and refusing to record it strands the atom `in_progress` forever. `ottoq_shield_probe` returns `would_block` and leaves enforcement to the caller, so advisory is the default, not a concession. | open |
| **G128** *(from [0419](db/migrations/0419_my_own_abstention_counter_counted_two_of_the_four_abstentions_as_real_verdicts.sql) §1 — self-inflicted, fixed same night)* | **The abstention counter shipped by 0418 counted two of the four abstentions as real verdicts.** The five codes at `task_completion` abstain in four independently-written phrasings — HW.006 *'insufficient context for presence verification'*, TW.002 *'insufficient context'*, SLA.003 *'missing context'*, SM.002 *'no transition context'* — and 0418 matched only `'insufficient context%'`. So `real_verdicts` was inflated and the `VACUOUS` verdict became unreachable for SLA.003 and SM.002: **a rule abstaining on every single call would have read "clean on real verdicts".** G120's defect class inside the instrument built to detect G120. Found by running it, not reading it. | `0419` V2 proves the new predicate on all seven observed phrasings; **V2b demonstrates the regression** by showing 0418's caught 2 of 4. | Fixed by `0419`: an abstention is a PASS whose reason mentions context. **Honest limit, stated in the result itself:** it is a phrase match over free text, so the function returns `abstention_is_heuristic`. The durable fix is a structured abstention signal on `ottoq_rule_result`, which changes every evaluator in the engine — named, not made. | closed |
| **G129** *(from [0419](db/migrations/0419_my_own_abstention_counter_counted_two_of_the_four_abstentions_as_real_verdicts.sql) §3)* | **The atom vocabulary and the declared task state machine disagree on the terminal word, and the disagreement is a trap.** At completion, SM.002's missing `from_state`/`to_state` looks obviously supplyable as `in_progress` → `done`. It is not: `ottoq_state_transitions` declares the task terminal as **`completed`**, so supplying the atom's own words would return `FALSE, 'invalid transition for task: in_progress → done'` at severity `critical` — **a false failure on every completion, manufactured from a word.** (`cancelled` happens to match; the terminal does not.) | `0419` P3 asserts `done` is still undeclared, so the reasoning stays checkable rather than resting on a note. | **Do not map `done`→`completed` inside a probe** — an atom is a jsonb element of `ottoq_visit_needs.atoms`, not a row of whatever `entity_kind='task'` describes, and translating would assert an identity nothing in the schema declares. Whether the atom lifecycle belongs in `ottoq_state_transitions` at all is a real question for 0412's work on that table. | open — declaration question |
| **G130** *(from [0327](db/checks/0327_the_value_check_is_still_unavailable_and_the_reason_is_now_a_unit_bug_in_my_own_fix.sql) §4 — self-inflicted, fixed by [0420](db/migrations/0420_i_converted_the_hazard_with_the_metronome_cadence_instead_of_the_sim_time_the_tick_actually_advances.sql))* | **`0413` re-denominated the fault hazard per eligible vehicle-hour and then converted it with the wrong duration.** `ottoq_sim_runs.tick_interval_seconds` is the REAL-TIME metronome cadence; it enters the sim-time advance only in the FIXED branch of `twin.ottoq_sim_advance_tick_world`, multiplied by `time_scale`. Measured from `ottoq_tick_clock_log.sim_advance_s` over 1,179 ticks: mean **7.90** sim-seconds against an assumed 30 → hazard **3.80x too high in `live` mode**; a `fixed`-mode tick advances `tick_interval_seconds * time_scale` = **1,800 s (30 min)** → **60x too low**. **Wrong in both modes and in opposite directions**, which is why no single run's outcomes could reveal it. Same defect class 0413 was written to close: a rate divided by a duration that is not the duration it elapses over. | `0327` §4, the per-tick log; `0420` V2 asserts the hazard now tracks the measured tick size to within 1%. | Fixed by `0420` (applied `20260922080856`): read `payload->>'tick_minutes_actual'`, which the world advancer already writes every tick under a comment saying it exists *"so every per-tick RATE cap downstream scales with the real tick size"* — the fault hazard was the one cap not reading it. A published advance of **zero is honoured, not coalesced**: live mode floors at 0 when no real time elapsed, and a tick in which the world did not age carries no hazard. | closed |
| **G131** *(from [0420](db/migrations/0420_i_converted_the_hazard_with_the_metronome_cadence_instead_of_the_sim_time_the_tick_actually_advances.sql) §3)* | **Certification was silently excluding the fault model it exists to certify.** `fixed` mode is what `run_by IN ('cert_harness','benchmark')` must use — `0258` refuses `live` for those so a cert's tick size cannot be wall-clock — and it is the mode G130 understated **60x**. A 48-tick fixed-mode cert spans 24 sim-hours and at ~100 eligible vehicles expected **0.035** faults: a canon covering a full simulated day encoded essentially zero vehicle failures. After `0420`, about **2**, which is Chase's ~1.5/day assumption plus the eligible share. | `0420` §3's two-mode table; `0327` §4. | `forces_recert` **TRUE** on `0420`, deliberately and expensively — every canon that encoded the suppressed rate encoded the defect. **Determinism is unaffected in fixed mode:** the advance there is a pure function of two stored run columns, so both arms of a pair compute the same hazard. Certs need re-running. | open — recert sweep owed |

| **G133** *(from [0327](db/checks/0327_the_value_check_is_still_unavailable_and_the_reason_is_now_a_unit_bug_in_my_own_fix.sql) §5, complementing G120)* | **The same rule, in the same run, is exact on the engine and blind to the seeder — so "SM.001 is green" and "SM.001 caught a defect" are both true and neither generalises.** `0324` §3 found SM.001 reading green over fifteen `offline -> charge_complete_holding` transitions because the seeding path never reaches its row-level trigger; measured at ten times the horizon, `from_state='offline'` is **still 0 of 949** against 15 such events, while the one transition inside the probe's population was caught on first occurrence. | `0327` §5's evaluation census with `from_offline` beside `failed`. | **Coverage and cleanliness are different properties** (G120), and this is the cleanest demonstration: one rule, one run, both behaviours. `0412` §4's promotion order still must be rewritten against the events census rather than SM.001's log. | open |
| **G134** *(from [0328](db/checks/0328_lambda_answered_the_model_lands_inside_chases_range_at_both_exposure_levels_measured.sql) §3 — RETRACTS exposure figures in G130's neighbours, self-inflicted)* | **The eligible-vehicle-hour measurement used by [0324](db/checks/0324_the_fault_fix_validated_on_live_traffic_and_a_green_sm001_is_not_evidence_of_no_undeclared_transitions.sql) §1, [0327](db/checks/0327_the_value_check_is_still_unavailable_and_the_reason_is_now_a_unit_bug_in_my_own_fix.sql) §2–§3 and 0328's first draft is NON-DETERMINISTIC on this data.** It orders a vehicle's `vehicle.state_changed` events by `sim_clock_at` and treats consecutive events as segment boundaries — but every event in a tick carries that tick's clock: **1,734 events over 49 distinct clock values (35.4 per value), of which 418 are the same vehicle changing state more than once inside one tick.** `ORDER BY sim_clock_at` is not a total order, so `row_number()` picks an arbitrary first state and `lead()` pairs arbitrary neighbours. **Three readings of the same frozen run gave 1,484.0, 1,577.0 and 1,269.5 eligible vehicle-hours — a 24% spread with the data unchanged.** | `0328` §3: the tie census beside the three readings; the corrected query re-run twice gives 1,269.50 both times. | Fixed, and the fix is **semantic not just a tiebreak**: a vehicle that goes A→B→C inside one tick spent **zero sim time in B**, since the tick is the world's time quantum. Collapse to one row per `(vehicle, sim_clock)` taking the LAST state (`DISTINCT ON … ORDER BY event_seq DESC`; the pre-first segment takes the FIRST `from`), then segment between distinct clocks. **The old method OVERSTATED exposure, so it UNDERSTATED implied faults/day.** Every ratio and conclusion in 0324/0327 survives — only absolute vehicle-hour figures move. | closed |
| **G135** *(from [0328](db/checks/0328_lambda_answered_the_model_lands_inside_chases_range_at_both_exposure_levels_measured.sql) §4 — closes the re-tune question)* | **λ needs no re-tuning, and the reason is that exposure is not a constant.** 0413 derived λ = 0.00088 by dividing an assumed 1.5 faults/day by **1,706.9** eligible vehicle-hours/sim-day taken from the contaminated baseline `d68d05bb`. Two clean measurements on G134's corrected method: **cert busy_day/171717 48-tick fixed = 45.6% eligible share, 1,269 vh/sim-day; demo busy_day/700001 live = 80.4%, 2,239 vh/sim-day.** Same scenario code, same depot, **exposure differs by 76%.** So 1,706.9 was not merely contaminated — it was the wrong KIND of number, a scenario-dependent quantity used as a constant, and replacing it with 1,269 would repeat the mistake on a cleaner input. | `0328` §4's two-run table. | **Nothing to change.** 0413 already chose the physically correct denominator — a hazard per eligible vehicle-hour, since a vehicle sitting in a depot state accrues risk while it sits — so faults/day is an OUTPUT that varies with exposure. At both measured exposures one untuned λ gives **1.12 and 1.97 faults/day against Chase's stated 1–2.** | closed |
| **G136** *(from [0328](db/checks/0328_lambda_answered_the_model_lands_inside_chases_range_at_both_exposure_levels_measured.sql) §2, §5, §6 — REWRITTEN; §5's own plan was impossible)* | **Pooled over the whole certified evidence base: 4,056.0 eligible vehicle-hours, 3.569 expected vehicle faults at λ, ZERO observed — P = 2.8%, with a 95% upper bound of 0.00074 that sits BELOW λ = 0.00088.** A marginal signal that the realised rate is low. Four things were verified before treating it as one, and all four are correct: the handler runs (both depots `feed_mode='sim'`; `production_live` runs do condemn); the hazard is exactly **4.399e-4**/tick = `1-exp(-0.00088 x 30/60)` for 0420's fixed-mode tick, multiplier 1; the exposure matches the handler's OWN eligibility read from source (`category='autonomous'`, `NOT jsonb_exists(config,'exception')`, the seven states — summing to exactly 1,269.5 on the 48-tick arm); and the RNG is uniform (5,568 draws, mean 0.50144, stddev 0.28705, 1 below the hazard vs 2.45 expected). **AND §5's plan to "pool ~5 48-tick arms" is impossible:** there is ONE 48-tick canon, and a deterministic engine on a fixed seed reproduces the identical fault count at every recert — repeated sweeps add nothing, and a pair's two arms are one draw, not two. The nine canon CELLS are the entire independent evidence base, permanently. | `0328` §6: the per-cell table, the four verification checks, and the RNG distribution test. | **Do not re-tune.** P = 2.8% is one-in-thirty-five from a single sweep, the deviation is in the operationally safe direction, and §4 shows exposure varies too much between scenarios for one λ to match every faults/day figure anyway. **More evidence requires NEW CANON CELLS at fresh seeds, not more sweeps** — two or three extra 48-tick cells would roughly triple pooled exposure. That is a `ottoq_determinism_canon` change plus a recert cost, so it is Chase's to authorise. | open |
| **G139** *(from [0328](db/checks/0328_lambda_answered_the_model_lands_inside_chases_range_at_both_exposure_levels_measured.sql) §6 — numerator trap, caught one query from publishing the opposite)* | **An intermediate count found 39 "fault events" in the cert arms and I briefly concluded λ was vindicated. They are all `charge.session_faulted` on `ocpp_session` entities — charger faults from an entirely different mechanism.** Vehicle faults are zero, confirmed three independent ways: no `tow_requested`, no vehicle gaining `config.exception` (diff shape verified rather than assumed), and none of the handler's own four event types (`ottoq.bay_eviction`, `ottoq.bay_eviction_deferred`, `vehicle.technician_approved`, `vehicle.tow_retrieved_staged`) appearing at all. | `0328` §6's event-type breakdown. | **Counting the wrong numerator is the same family as counting the wrong denominator** — G125, G130, G134 are the denominator instances from the same two days. A `LIKE '%fault%'` filter is not a definition of the event you mean. | closed || **G137** *(from [0329](db/checks/0329_every_certified_pair_disagrees_when_you_recompute_it_from_the_evidence_it_was_certified_on.sql) — the sharpest reproducibility finding to date)* | **All nine pairs certified 2026-09-22 recorded `equal=true` with `disagreeing_atoms = {}`. Recompute atom 4 (`events`) from the stored rows today and ALL NINE DISAGREE.** In every pair arm A carries exactly one extra `vehicle.state_changed` event **per vehicle at that depot** (116 at the twin, 4 at the `grid_smoke` fixture), all at arm A's FINAL `sim_clock_at`, all SoC-shaped with **no `current_state` in the diff** — while the transition population is **identical** (1,734 in both arms of pair 9). **Cause, from source:** `ottoq_determinism_pair` runs both arms in ONE transaction and calls `ottoq_tick_invariance_reset_fleet` at position 880 against `twin.ottoq_sim_start_run` at 954 — i.e. **before the arm's run exists** — while `ottoq.ottoq_active_sim_run_id()` caches its answer in a **transaction-local** GUC that the pair never clears. So arm 2's fleet reset is filed as arm 1's evidence, stamped at arm 1's last tick. | `0329` §1 the 9/9/0/9 recomputation using `h_evt` verbatim from the pair function (no filter, so a row-count delta cannot hide); §2 the per-pair delta table; §3 the comment-stripped positional proof. | **WRITTEN AND PROVEN, NOT APPLIED: [0421](db/migrations/0421_a_certified_arms_evidence_is_rewritten_by_the_next_arms_fleet_reset.sql).** One statement — `PERFORM set_config('ottoq.sim_run_id','none',true)` before each arm's reset; `'none'` is the sentinel `ottoq_active_sim_run_id` maps to NULL, so harness setup belongs to no run. **Cause AND fix both demonstrated in a rolled-back transaction on the 4-vehicle fixture depot: the stale cache files +4 events into a certified arm, the sentinel files +0.** The displaced events land cleanly rather than relocating the problem — `sim_run_id IS NULL`, `data_source='twin'`, `ingest_source='trigger'`, distinguishable from production telemetry by 2.8's own discipline. Installed by surgical substitution (match count 1, byte delta exactly the two literals'), with V2 naming the 0152/0175/0258/0386 guards that must survive. `forces_recert` TRUE — `events` is one of the fourteen, so all nine canons re-run (~36 min). Does NOT repair the nine contaminated arms — `ottoq_events` is append-only and deleting rows to make a verdict reproduce is backwards. **APPLIED 2026-09-22 `20260922125637` and VERIFIED by [0330](db/checks/0330_the_repaired_arm_a_hashes_to_what_arm_b_always_was_so_the_fix_removed_the_contamination_and_nothing_else.sql):** recomputing atom 4 from stored rows, the 3 pairs recertified since the floor read **3 agree / 0 disagree**, against **0 / 9** for the nine before it. Per-arm event totals: delta 4 → 0 (fixture), 116 → 0 (twin). | **CLOSED** 2026-09-22 13:39 UTC — the sweep landed all nine and `db/checks/0330` §1 now reads **9 pairs / 9 said clean / 9 AGREE today / 0 disagree**, against 0/9 for the nine before the floor. |
| **G138** *(from [0329](db/checks/0329_every_certified_pair_disagrees_when_you_recompute_it_from_the_evidence_it_was_certified_on.sql) §5 — the distinction to protect)* | **Every verdict is correct and none is re-derivable, and those are different claims.** Each `h_evt` was computed before the next arm's reset existed, so all nine comparisons ran over clean, complete, equal sets — determinism is not in question and neither is 0420. What fails is **auditability**: a reviewer recomputing from the archive today finds nine of nine failing on atom 4. **So CLAUDE.md 2.9a's sentence — "same inputs, byte-identical outputs, verified continuously, across fourteen independent atoms" — describes a LIVE check and must not be offered as something a reviewer can re-derive from stored rows.** That is the gap between "we verified it" and "you can verify it", and the second is what the claim is worth under hostile diligence. | `0329` §5. Also checked and clean: [0328](db/checks/0328_lambda_answered_the_model_lands_inside_chases_range_at_both_exposure_levels_measured.sql)'s λ figures, which reconstruct exposure only from transition-carrying events — **every contaminating row fails that filter**, both arms read 1,734, so λ stands. | **Fourth instance of one class, and the inverse of the other three:** 0137 hashed a write timestamp, 0139 was id-blind only after a fix, 0216 hashed a `uuid_generate_v4()` session id — each a fingerprint spoiled by something that is not the thing fingerprinted. **Here the fingerprint is sound and the EVIDENCE moves underneath it**, which no amount of care in the digest can catch. | **permanent** — 0421 fixes it forward; the nine contaminated arms stay non-re-derivable because `ottoq_events` is append-only and the rows were mutated in place. Nothing to repair, only to supersede. |

| **G140** *(from [0330](db/checks/0330_the_repaired_arm_a_hashes_to_what_arm_b_always_was_so_the_fix_removed_the_contamination_and_nothing_else.sql) §3 — the by-product, and it is the property 2.9a actually sells)* | **The repaired arm A hashes to EXACTLY the value pre-fix arm B has carried since 09:34, in all three recertified columns.** Three of the four `h_evt` digests in each (scenario, seed, ticks) group are identical; the only outlier is pre-fix arm A. Two consequences: (a) **0421 is exactly the right size** — it deleted the contaminating rows and perturbed nothing, where a fix that changed behaviour would have moved all four digests to some new common value; (b) **this is the first CROSS-TRANSACTION determinism datum in the engine's life.** `ottoq_determinism_pair` only ever proves A == B *inside one transaction* — same snapshot, same backend, same connection. Here the same digest appears in a pair certified at **09:34** and one at **12:59**: different runs, transactions, backends, 3.5 hours apart, engine hash changed in between. | `0330` §3's four-row-per-column digest table. | **Do not promote this to a claim on this evidence** — three columns, one morning, one database. | **G147** *(from [0335](db/checks/0335_the_walkaround_owns_96_percent_of_the_bay_calendar_and_displaces_nothing_that_can_be_demonstrated.sql) §4 — a defect in my own query, caught before publishing, twice)* | **`<>` IS NOT `IS DISTINCT FROM`, and on this table it changes a count of unfinished work by a factor of 242.** Counting unfinished mandatory bay atoms with `FILTER (WHERE status <> 'done')` returns **4**; with `IS DISTINCT FROM` it returns **970**, because **966 of the 1,374 atoms carry `status IS NULL`** and `NULL <> 'done'` evaluates to NULL, which `FILTER` drops. The wrong form reads as 99.7% completion. | `0335` §4, both counts over identical rows in one query. | **And the near-miss afterwards is the part to keep:** applying `0334`'s brand-new partition rule moved 99.7% to 29.4% and I briefly read that as the partition having caught it. It had not — both figures are the same day. **The partition was innocent and the operator was guilty**, and crediting the wrong fix would have left the real one in place. **RULE: a count of things that did NOT happen must use `IS DISTINCT FROM`**, because "did not happen" is the state most likely to be stored as NULL. Every `FILTER (WHERE x <> '…')` in this repo is suspect for the same reason. **AUDIT DONE, same session (`0335` §5): THE ENGINE IS CLEAN.** 125 database functions use `<>`; **2** use it inside a `FILTER`, and **0** can fail open. `ottoq_twin_determinism_verdict` — the function that decides whether two arms disagree, where this bug would have been worst — is **explicitly NULL-guarded on both sides** (`ma IS NOT NULL AND mb IS NOT NULL AND ha <> hb`). `ottoq_rules_headline` was settled by arithmetic rather than by reading source: its buckets sum (**26 + 4 = 30 = registered_active**), so no NULL is being dropped. Of the five `ottoq_assert*` sites, none is in a FILTER and none fails open (a `count(*)`, a `NOT NULL` column with 0 nulls in 1,746 rows, an explicit `IS NOT NULL` guard, two `COALESCE`'d arrays, and one early-return guard that fails in the SAFE direction). **The failure was mine, ad hoc, and never reached a migration or a stored function — that is the blast radius.** **The reusable guard is arithmetic, not vigilance:** assert that a partition's buckets sum to its population and a NULL cannot hide in the gap — the same instrument as `0417`'s `classes_sum_to_calls` and `0341`. | closed — audited, engine clean, guard recorded |

| **G143** *(from [0332](db/checks/0332_the_tick_never_blocked_on_the_agent_and_the_real_cost_is_that_three_of_four_calls_are_discarded.sql) — a retraction of my own sentence, found while opening G62 step 4 to build it)* | **CLAUDE.md said "the tick blocks a mean of 19.7 seconds of its 30-second beat on advice." THE TICK DOES NOT BLOCK ON THE AGENT AND NEVER DID.** `ottoq_sim_decide_and_dispatch` fires it with `net.http_post(..., timeout_milliseconds := 20000)` — pg_net is fire-and-forget — and the function holds **no `pg_sleep`, no read of `net._http_response`, and no loop** across all 6,506 characters. The timeout bounds the *worker’s* request, not the caller. **The 19.7 s was never measured; it was inferred from a latency column** that answers "how long did the agent take", not "what waited for it". **So G62 step 4 is already done and must be struck rather than scheduled.** **And the real defect is larger: 2,735 of 3,680 live agent calls — 74.3% — end `handoff_status='fallback'`, computed and discarded.** **Not latency:** the discarded calls average **19,308 ms** against **22,805 ms** for the applied ones — 3.5 s *faster* — and none of the 2,735 carries a `solver_handoff.receipt.tick_seq` while all 936 applied ones do, so the fallback is decided upstream of the apply. | `0332` §1 the three source assertions, comment-stripped; §2 the handoff-status table with latencies; §3 staleness re-derived live (mean **4.20** ticks, **2.14%** changed answers against a **25.48%** floor — the half of the old sentence that survives, and stronger); §2b the dial both halves gate on. | **Open, cause not established, and 0332 says so rather than guessing.** The candidate is `agent_solver_chain_enabled`: the tick fires the agent only when it is BELOW 1 and `ottoq_agent_solver_refresh` refuses the solver chain when it is BELOW 1 — two halves of a switch that may never have been reconciled. That is a reading of two source fragments, not a measurement. **Sixth instance of one shape** (0329 a hash over a minted id, 0413 a rate over scheduler ticks, 0326 §1 a calendar gate against the wrong clock, 0331 an actor inferred from whether a run is live): a real measurement read as answering a question it was not about. **A duration is not a wait.** | **RETRACTED IN ITS HEADLINE 2 HOURS LATER — see G146.** The 74.3% is a lifetime average over a deployment outage already fixed; the chain completed 577 of its last 579 calls. The half that stands is that the tick does not block on the agent. |

| **G148** *(from [0336](db/checks/0336_the_calendar_cannot_say_who_made_two_thirds_of_its_own_entries.sql) — found trying to answer a one-join question from `0335` §6)* | **~~`ottoq_stall_bookings` cannot say who made 20,225 of its 31,730 twin-depot entries.~~ RETRACTED 2026-09-22 by [0338](db/checks/0338_the_calendar_names_the_writer_of_every_single_row_and_g148_said_it_could_not.sql): IT NAMES A WRITER ON EVERY ROW.** The calendar carries **five** provenance columns and I measured the only sparse one. On 36,268 twin-depot bookings: `booked_by` **0 NULL** (and `NOT NULL` in the schema, so it cannot be otherwise), `why` **0 NULL with 23,118 distinct per-booking English explanations**, `need_source` **0 NULL**, `leg_source` **0 NULL** — and `source` 63.7% NULL. What is true is narrower: **the calendar's attribution has two resolutions and the `source` column covers a third of it.** And the SPREAD is the finding, not the total: **`temp_hold` (8,190) and `perimeter_hold` (3,527) are 100% unattributed with ZERO distinct sources ever recorded**, `service` 94.0%, `wash` 79.2%, `detail` 82.3%, `charge_l2` 37.5%, `charge_dcfc` 17.4% — while **`inspect` (3,837) and `staging` (456) are 0% unattributed.** Some writers instrument themselves and others never have; the ones that never have are the holds, 37% of the calendar. | `0336` §1 the by-purpose table; §2 the walkaround join that does not close (2,603 of 2,763 name nobody, and four of the six named sources first appear today); §3 the calendar at 63.7% unattributed against `ottoq_model_call_ledger` at **0.0%**. | **It is not data loss** — every booking is present and the EXCLUDE constraint is untouched. It is the booking-table analogue of the rule this company runs on: *"no number ships without a run ID"* is honoured (every booking carries `sim_run_id`); **"no booking ships without a writer" has no analogue**, and the cost is exactly what `0335` hit — a live 2,754-row behaviour on the depot’s scarcest resource with no way to ask the table which code did it. **The comparison that makes the case:** `ottoq_model_call_ledger` (`0340`) knows its provider and source kind on 100% of rows, which is precisely why `0334` could partition a three-day outage out of a lifetime average in one query this morning. **FIX ORDER — CORRECTED by `0338` §2, because `0336` named the wrong function.** `ottoq_record_enacted_booking` **already has `p_source` and already works on 12,868 of 12,868 rows** (`booked_by='otto_q_enacted'`, 0 NULL, 16 distinct sources) — it is the exemplar, not the defect. The gap is `ottoq.ottoq_book_stall`, **the only direct `INSERT INTO ottoq_stall_bookings` in the database**, which has no `p_source` parameter at all and omits `source` from its INSERT column list, so its callers cannot name themselves. Give **that** function a `p_source` and let its four callers name themselves — **not** a `NOT NULL` on a column with 20,225 existing nulls, which forces a backfill nobody can verify. **Do not backfill:** a guessed `source` is a number without a run ID. Then `0335` §6 becomes answerable. **Not measured (§5):** whether `source` is CORRECT where present. | **headline RETRACTED (`0338`); the narrower `source`-coverage finding is open and NO LONGER BLOCKS the walkaround fix** — `why` answered it outright (see G152). **~~AND THE SAME SHAPE ON A SECOND TABLE (`0336` §6): G121 IS UNWATCHABLE.~~ RETRACTED ON EVERY CLAUSE 2026-09-22 by [0341](db/checks/0341_g121_was_never_unwatchable_and_the_archive_says_the_transition_that_creates_it_never_happened.sql).** The diff is a GENERIC whole-row `ottoq_jsonb_diff`, so **six** distinct keys exist and it carries `reserved_by` on **63,616** events, `current_vehicle_id` on **53,720**, `status` on **53,720** — with `{from, to}` on both sides — and **the exact pair that defines G121 on 49,140 events.** Not one payload key, not only `status`, not structurally absent. I had measured `new_state`, found it NULL on 92.1%, and concluded the row was not stored — but that column is emptied **by design** by `ottoq_events_slim_new_state_bi`, which keeps one anchor snapshot per `(run, event_type, entity, day)` and nulls the rest because *"the diff carries the news"*. (Contrast `vehicle.state_changed`, whose diff carries SoC, `target_soc`, `config` and `last_state_change` — `0329` reconstructed a whole contamination from it.) **Also recorded, because it is `0334`'s error with the sign reversed:** checking G121 on an idle depot returns "all 158 clean" and means nothing. ~~**Add the two pointer fields to the stall trigger's diff BEFORE fixing G121.**~~ **THAT PREREQUISITE IS FABRICATED AND WITHDRAWN** — the fields are already there, and it blocked a live defect on the depot's scarcest resource behind a migration that never needed to exist. **A fabricated blocker is worse than a wrong number, because it stops work rather than merely misdescribing it.** **AND THE CORRECT INSTRUMENT RETURNS SOMETHING SHARPER:** of **26,860** events moving `status` to `available`, **ZERO** leave `current_vehicle_id` set; and **ZERO** events change `current_vehicle_id` without also changing `status` (the two appear on exactly 53,720 events each — they never move independently). **So G121's three `dcfc` stalls never reached that state through this trigger**, and the churn filter is checked and does not explain it — which points at a writer reaching `stalls` outside the signed event stream. That is a more serious finding than G121 and is the open question now; WHICH writer is not established. |
| **G149** *(from [0337](db/checks/0337_the_shield_says_blocked_seven_thousand_times_and_blocks_nothing_at_six_of_nine_probe_points.sql) §3 — found while writing down a number that turned out to mean the opposite of what it reads)* | **`ottoq_rule_evaluations.enforcement_taken='blocked'` is a RECOMMENDATION THE CALLER MAY DISCARD, and six of the nine probe-point callers discard it. 5,721 of the ledger's 7,626 `blocked` rows — 75.0% — record a refusal that never happened** (twin depot: 5,648). All nine callers reach the shield through `public.ottoq_shield_probe`, which cannot raise — it RETURNS a `would_block` column. **Three read it** and branch on `IF COALESCE(v_blocks,0) > 0`: `ottoq_enact_inspection_seam` (task_start, 1,268 blocks), `ottoq_shield_and_log` (stall_assignment AND redeployment, 611), `ottoq_decide_tick` (bess_dispatch, 26). **Six call it with `PERFORM`, which discards the rows by definition**: task_completion (1,042), charge_session_start (4,664), bess_state_change (15), vehicle_state_change, stall_state_change, policy_write. One column name means "the engine refused this" at three probe points and "a rule objected and nobody asked" at six, with nothing in the row to tell them apart. | `0337` §3 the caller-by-caller source table and the blocked-row census cross-tabulated against it; §4 the independent witness (all 3 BESS units in `standby`, 2,451 walkaround atoms `done`) that the discarded verdicts did not stop the writes. | **Not a bug to fix by turning enforcement on** — see G150, which is why the discard is load-bearing. The fix is to the LEDGER: `enforcement_taken` needs to distinguish "refused" from "would have objected", so a reader cannot take one for the other. `ottoq_shield_and_log` serving two probe points through a variable `v_ac` is also why a literal search finds no caller for `redeployment` at all — same lesson as `0326` §6(a). | **open** — measured, not yet fixed. Note what is UNCHANGED: `0326` §6's 34-of-69 pairs and the 96.9% enacted-decision coverage measure whether a rule is CONSULTED; this is the first measurement of whether its answer is READ. |
| **G150** *(from [0337](db/checks/0337_the_shield_says_blocked_seven_thousand_times_and_blocks_nothing_at_six_of_nine_probe_points.sql) §5 — the reason G149 must not be "fixed" by enabling enforcement)* | **Two of the shield's critical rules fail BY CONSTRUCTION on the twin, and the non-enforcing wiring at their probe points is the only reason the twin still runs.** (a) **`HW.002.charger_state_precondition` (critical/block), 4,632 twin-depot blocks — and the rule CANNOT PASS, proven by zero variance.** Differencing the rule's own recorded `now_ts` against its own `last_heartbeat_at` across all 4,632 failures gives **min = max = 1800 s, exactly one distinct value**, against `max_offline_seconds = 90`. 1800 s is one tick: both tick functions write `last_heartbeat_at = <tick sim clock>` once per tick (gated on `depots.feed_mode='sim'`, and the twin IS `'sim'`), and **the charge-session start runs earlier in the tick than that write**, so it always reads the previous tick's value. `max_offline_seconds=90` is a real-world OCPP interval; the twin's cadence is 1800 sim-seconds — **a 20x units mismatch**, same class as the fault-hazard units defect. (b) **`HW.003.sensor_liveness` (safety_critical/block), 808 blocks, all `perimeter_walkaround`** — staleness reads 600/660/720/780/840/900 s against a 300 s threshold, i.e. the atom's own elapsed duration; the walkaround is declared at 720 s (`0383`) and every shorter service passes **1,496 of 1,496**. | `0337` §5 the 45-charger census and the HW.003 staleness histogram; §2 the service-level split showing the failures are confined to one service. **`0337` §5(a) also records this file's own first draft being wrong**: I wrote that the heartbeat column was *frozen, twenty days stale*, having compared a SIM date (2026-09-02, the last canon run's final sim clock) against the real calendar — i.e. `0326` §1's defect, committed in a paragraph that disclaimed it. The zero-variance check is what caught it. | **Order matters:** fix the rule INPUTS first — for HW.002 either move the heartbeat write ahead of the decide path or denominate `max_offline_seconds` in the tick clock (**a units question to answer, not a threshold to tune**); ~~for HW.003 give it a sensor age that is not the task's own duration~~ **— RETRACTED by [0340](db/checks/0340_hw003_is_a_charge_only_rule_that_never_learns_the_service_because_my_probe_spells_the_key_differently.sql), fixed by [0426](db/migrations/0426_a_charge_only_rule_fails_every_walkaround_because_my_probe_spells_the_service_key_svc_and_it_reads_service.sql). HW.003 is CHARGE-ONLY by its own scope guard and the cause is a context key: the probe passes `svc`, the evaluator reads `service_code`/`service`. Live probe, same vehicle/threshold/clock: `svc` → fails *"SOC sensor stale: 1821142 seconds old"*; `service_code` → passes *"non-charging action (perimeter_walkaround): SOC liveness N/A"*. `task_start` carries `service` on **69,514** evaluations with **zero** failures, which settles the convention; `task_completion` is the only probe point spelling it differently and the only one that fails. The staleness arithmetic explained the NUMBER, not the VERDICT — a threshold cannot cause a verdict a scope guard should have prevented. Fix is one added key (`svc` KEPT, not renamed); predicted effect stated before applying: HW.003 becomes VACUOUS at this probe point, so four of five codes are vacuous instead of three**, confirm the failure rates go to zero, and only then promote the probe. That is 2.9a's blind-spot doctrine applied to enforcement instead of to atoms. Enforcing first would stop the twin charging and stop it completing walkarounds. | **(a) FIXED, applied `20260922150552` — [0424](db/migrations/0424_a_charger_heartbeat_written_after_the_charge_starts_is_a_liveness_check_that_cannot_pass.sql). (b) open.** `0424` stamps the tick's heartbeats BEFORE the tick's charge reconciliation in both orchestrators; the late write stays and becomes an idempotent rewrite (V2 asserts two writes per orchestrator, so a statement was added, not moved). Verified from the catalog independently of the migration's own asserts: heartbeat position 2,190 vs charge work 2,434 in `twin.ottoq_world_advance` and 2,982 vs 3,103 in `public.ottoq_sim_advance_tick_world` — both were the other way round before. **V3 is the assertion that matters**: HW.002 must still be ABLE to fail, so it probes the live faulted charger `B-NASH-L2-29` at ZERO staleness (`now_ts` := its own heartbeat) and requires the refusal to name the station state — otherwise the check would pass on the liveness branch and test nothing. `forces_recert` TRUE; floor moved to 15:06:22 and 9 of 9 canons went pending. **NOT YET VERIFIED and stated as such: the post-apply failure rate, and `distinct_gaps > 1`, which is the real signal** — a rate can fall for many reasons, but the gap losing its zero variance can only mean the ordering changed. (b) is unchanged and is the same defect `0333` called on 102 failures, holding at 808. |

Both firing codes are G150 tautologies, and HW.006's are downstream of the phantom bay booking already open as the walkaround fix. | **confirmed** — G144's retraction holds at nine times the evidence. |
| **G152** *(from [0338](db/checks/0338_the_calendar_names_the_writer_of_every_single_row_and_g148_said_it_could_not.sql) §5–§6 — the answer G148 was wrongly believed to be blocking)* | **Nothing in the database reads `service_cadence_policy.lane_stalls`, the column that says whether a service occupies a stall — and the walkaround books 3,257 bookings on the twin depot's TWO service bays for work performed at the vehicle.** The `why` column names the mechanism in English on every row: *"NASH-SVC-01 service 11:08-11:23 to satisfy need 'perimeter_walkaround' (visit_atom); leg 8cb99275 (service, matched caller)"* — a service leg books a bay per atom. **The anomaly isolates to a single pair with lane, `lane_stalls` and concurrency all held constant:** `sensor_clean` (exterior / NULL / exterior) books **0** stalls; `perimeter_walkaround` (exterior / NULL / exterior) books **3,257** — and `0383` re-derived the walkaround as `concurrency='exterior'` *explicitly modelled on `sensor_clean`*. | **G153** *(from [0339](db/checks/0339_pooling_certified_arms_multiplies_exposure_and_adds_no_information_and_my_own_check_in_told_me_to.sql) — found by following a scheduled instruction I had written myself, and checking its arithmetic before publishing it)* | **A determinism pair cannot generate independent samples, so pooling certified arms multiplies exposure and adds no information — and nothing in the data marks the duplicates.** Measured: **50 arm rows since `0420` against 7 independent observations** (`count(DISTINCT (scenario_code, random_seed, tick_count, depot_id))`), and all six 48-tick arms are ONE canon cell — `busy_day`/171717/48, three pairs at 09:58, 13:26, 14:12, one enabled 48-tick column. Two reasons, and the second is the interesting one: within a pair the arms are byte-identical **by construction**; across pairs the repetitions are identical **because that is the property 2.9a sells** — same seed, same scenario, same engine hash, and a twin whose RNG is a pure hash of `(seed, entity, sim-seconds-since-start)`. If the column drew zero faults at 09:58 it is *required* to draw zero at 14:12. | `0339` §1 the one-cell grouping and the canon's single 48-tick column; §1b the 50-vs-7 count; §2 zero condemn transitions across all 50 arms. | **The concrete near-miss:** my own check-in instructed *"pool their eligible vehicle-hours... ~5 arms give ~5.6 expected faults"*. | **G141** *(from [0330](db/checks/0330_the_repaired_arm_a_hashes_to_what_arm_b_always_was_so_the_fix_removed_the_contamination_and_nothing_else.sql) §5–§6 — found by trying to monitor the sweep)* | **A determinism pair blocks every other pg_cron job for its entire duration, and the missed firings are dropped rather than deferred.** Measured over 7 days: **64 long (>60 s) runs of job 746, ZERO firings of jobs 10 / 12 / 17 in their interiors, 64 of 64** — 5h 28m of cron outage inside a 40h 40m span, **13.6% of wall clock**. Only `pg_stat_activity` is honest. | `0330` §5 the three-monitor comparison; §6 the 64-run interior/burst table. | **Latency nuisance, not a safety hole — checked, not assumed.** The run governor is starved, but so is the metronome, and `ottoyarddepot-sim/src/hooks/useTwinControl.ts` states in its own comment that there is **NO BROWSER TICK LOOP** (the client loop was removed for colliding with the metronome's run lock), so nothing else advances a demo run: a frozen run cannot run away. On release a `live` run takes the whole frozen span in ONE oversized tick, and **0420 is what keeps that honest** — hazard scales on `tick_minutes_actual`, so a 3-minute tick gets 3 minutes of hazard. **The residual is a caveat on real-clock latency:** an agent-latency percentile taken from a window overlapping a sweep is not measuring the agent. | open |

| **G116** *(**[0403](db/migrations/0403_the_dials_outlive_their_run_so_reanalysis_becomes_possible_g116.sql)**, APPLIED `20260921172038`, from [0313](db/checks/0313_i_told_chase_the_cockpit_was_a_facade_and_it_is_live_the_learning_loop_is_the_one_that_has_never_run.sql) §3. Chase asked for self-improvement / reanalysis; this is the prerequisite, and the reason it had to come first.)* | **OTTO-Q cannot learn from itself, and the blocker is a missing INPUT rather than a missing algorithm.** `ottoq_prime` has set dials **1,351 times across 6 keys and every single write is `scope_type='run'`** — zero depot, zero global — so `ottoq_purge_prior_runs` deletes the agent's choice before anything can correlate it with what it produced. Measured: **0 of 1,526 archived runs record a dial.** The OUTCOME half was already durable and better than expected — `ottoq_run_archives` (1,526 rows, `class='run_ledger'`) carries `metrics`, `engine_hash`, `config_hash` and **`run_payload.tick_minutes_actual`, the per-run clock the *verdict* tables lacked** (`0402`). And comparability is genuinely strong: **198 cells** of engine_hash × scenario × clock, **130 with n≥2**, **52 with n≥5**, the largest **n=408 across 8 seeds** at one clock and one policy. **So the substrate was one column short.** | `0313` §3 (1,351/1,351 run-scoped; 6 param groups all v1/seed/`last_tuned_at` NULL; `prediction_outcomes`=1) and `0403`'s header (the 198/130/52/408 cell census and the 0-of-1,526 dial count) | **`0403` makes the choice outlive the run that made it, and is APPLIED.** `ottoq_run_dial_ledger` (`class='evidence'`, registered, **no FK to `ottoq_sim_runs`** by `0340`'s precedent — the run it describes is purged while the row remains) + an **error-swallowing AFTER INSERT trigger on the archive**, which fires in **the one window where dials and outcome coexist**: archiving happens at run END, the purge at the NEXT run's START. Plus `ottoq_dial_outcome_reanalysis` and `ottoq_assert_dial_ledger_readiness()`. **Deliberately NOT an edit to `ottoq_archive_run`** — it runs inside certified pairs and its `run_payload` already carries `world_fingerprint`; widening a certified path for a reporting goal is how a cert round breaks. `forces_recert` **FALSE** on `0402`'s measurable ground: a table, a trigger writing only that table, and a view, none of which any atom reads. | **open, and what remains is a product decision, not engineering.** **PROVEN, not assumed** — an error-swallowing trigger that silently writes nothing is this repo's signature defect, so a rolled-back probe archived a synthetic run and confirmed the trigger wrote **1 row** with dials `{deploy_peak_fraction: 0.85, energy_demand_factor_peak: 0.55}`, clock **30.0** from `tick_minutes_actual`, and **2** reanalysis rows; residue afterwards **0**. **`0146`'s TRAP IS DESIGNED OUT RATHER THAN NOTED:** `rule_evaluations` and `rules_blocked` travel in the **same row** as throughput, and the view exposes **`every_run_paid_the_shield`** beside every throughput column plus **`has_replication`**, so a one-seed cell cannot be read as a result and a cell that skipped the shield cannot be compared to one that paid it. **THERE IS NO REWARD FUNCTION, no ranking, no recommendation and no writer of dials** — which outcome is *better* is Chase's call, and a learner that wrote dials would be an AI changing engine state on the one path the L1 shield does not gate (rule 6's standing finding). **AND THE IRREVERSIBILITY IS THE FINDING:** readiness reads `captured=0` and **the 1,526 already-archived runs can never be backfilled**, because the dials they used were purged with them. **Every run completed before 17:20 UTC today is permanently unlearnable**, which is the argument for landing capture before anything else. **PART TWO IS DONE — [0404](db/migrations/0404_the_reward_function_and_dial_promotion_behind_hard_evidence_gates_g116.sql), APPLIED `20260921174317`: the reward is wired and dial promotion is ENABLED**, both on Chase's instruction. **AND IT CORRECTS THIS ROW'S OWN CLAIM.** The line above says a learner writing dials would be *"an AI changing engine state on the one path the L1 shield does not gate"* — that **conflated two paths**. `AI.001.agent_dial_within_envelope` is a **critical** rule at probe `policy_write` with **280 evaluations, 280 passed**, so a dial write IS watched; rule 6's ungated path is the **Nemotron advisory call**. **But not overcorrected:** AI.001 is `enforcement='log_only'`, so a dial write is **audited and catalog-bounded, not blocked** — the real refusal is `ottoq_policy_set`'s `{"ok":false}` contract (G65). AI.001 also **passes a non-agent actor unjudged**, so the promoter writes as **`ottoq_prime:promoter`**, inside the agent family on purpose, to be judged rather than exempt. **THE REWARD** is the product's own five KPIs via `ottoq_kpi_five`, normalised min-max **within** a comparability cell (engine_hash × scenario × clock), weighted by a **versioned** `ottoq_reward_weights` row recorded on every score. A confirmation that cannot fail is not a confirmation. **KPI #2 is EXONERATED and that must be said as plainly as the finding:** `ottoq_release_expired_bookings` distinguishes `window_elapsed` (released, never occupied) from `window_elapsed_occupied` (done, occupied), and `ottoq_kpi_service_point_turns` counts only the latter — the turn is real, the SERVICE is what did not happen, which a turns metric cannot see by construction. **RETRACTED AND RESTATED, SAME DAY.** The headline does not survive a per-run split, which is what I should have done first. Measured by run: the full-length operator demo **`f13fc580` (1,136 ticks) shows 84 of 84 occupied charge turns WITH a charging session — zero without**, while the entire no-session population sits in the 12/24/48-tick `cert_harness` pairs at ~50% each, with paired arms agreeing exactly (85/40/45 twice, 183/109/74 twice). **The initiator I failed to look for is `twin.ottoq_sim_confirm_commands`**, which writes `current_state` on command confirmation — and it was in the writer census I had already pulled; I read that list for the reconciler and not for the initiator. **Worse, the defect I "found" is documented in that function as already found and fixed**, in a comment more exact than mine: *"Migration 0039 moved the state write out of ottoq_decide_tick and into the twin on command confirmation — and this function never implemented that half… The chain was cut in the middle: measured 135 begin_charge executed, 0 ocpp_sessions."* So the chain is decide_tick → `begin_charge` carrying `new_state` → confirm_commands sets the state → the reconciler opens the session; "structurally unable" is true of the reconciler alone and false of the engine. **WHAT SURVIVES is smaller and still real:** all nine determinism canon columns are cert pairs, so **the canon certifies a world in which about half the charge turns never charge** — the G25/G28 shape, a comparison narrower than what it is trusted to cover, and no atom notices because both arms are equally inert. Two candidate causes to separate: truncation (~6 sim-minutes against a 31–44 minute charge window) versus the arm fixtures seating vehicles directly (`ottoq_tick_invariance_reset_fleet` calls `ottoq_sim_start_charge_session` itself). **Untouched by the retraction:** `ottoq_replan_after_charger_fault` reads the pointer and the charger but not the calendar. **The lesson:** refusing to publish a magnitude because the counts drifted is what stopped a wrong claim carrying a number, but drift was the symptom — the disease was aggregating across runs at all. *Cite the run, never the table* is advice about POPULATIONS, not freshness. **MECHANISM FOUND 2026-09-21 16:0x ([0308](db/checks/0308_the_fault_reroute_exists_and_the_charger_a_vehicle_is_parked_on_may_never_start_charging_it.sql) §8), and BOTH causes I proposed were wrong.** Not truncation (**0** windows outlast the run) and not fixture seating (**0** bookings start at or before the run clock). **It is a clock.** The `begin_charge` confirm lag on run `b4d5f76d` is **min 00:30:00, max 00:30:00 — always exactly one tick** — and that run is 48 ticks over 1 day of sim, i.e. **30 sim-MINUTES per tick**. So the confirm→state→session chain costs one tick, and a charge booking whose window is shorter than a tick expires `window_elapsed_occupied` — counted as a completed turn — before its session can start. **Average window WITH a session 70.8 min; WITHOUT, 28.4 min; 53 of 74 no-session bookings have windows shorter than the lag.** Three rows read in full show it outright: windows `02:30→02:41/02:44/02:45`, command issued `02:30`, **executed `03:00`** — correct payload, correct stall, correct `new_state`, arriving after the booking is gone. **AND IT EXPLAINS THE REAL RUN:** tick granularity is not a constant — `cert_harness` 30 min, `production_live` 2 min, `operator_demo` (`f13fc580`) **29 seconds**. One tick of latency against a 30–45 min charge window is negligible at 29 s and fatal at 30 min. Same engine, same path, opposite outcome, one variable. **TWO BROKEN WITNESSES CAME FIRST and neither was interpreted:** the command probe read **zero for BOTH groups** including the 109 that charged, because I compared `created_at` (WALL clock, identical for all 1,689 rows — a cert pair writes them in one transaction) against a SIM-clock range. **That is G107's own defect class, in my instrument rather than the engine.** **STILL OPEN:** the **21 of 74** the lag does not explain — a mechanism covering 72% is a mechanism, not the whole story; and the decision about the nine canon columns all running at 30 min/tick, which means **the canon certifies a world where the charging chain is structurally half inert** (no verdict invalidated — both arms equally inert, so byte-identical holds — but it is the G25/G28 shape with a mechanism attached). **Bears on C5:** an A/B rig at that granularity would measure the tick, not the policy. **THE RESIDUE IS NOW CLOSED, AND IT CLOSED BY SPLITTING IN TWO — [0311](db/checks/0311_the_twentyone_were_never_one_mechanism_and_the_calendar_IS_enforced_where_i_said_nothing_enforced_it.sql).** All **183** occupied charge turns on `b4d5f76d` decompose with no remainder: **2** had a session with no `begin_charge` command at all, **94** executed and charged, **65** executed and did not, **10** were refused and charged anyway, **12** were refused and did not. **MECHANISM ONE is the one-tick lag, but §8 NAMED THE WRONG VARIABLE.** The deciding term is not window length, it is **budget left** (`upper(during) − issued_at`): **70.0 min** for the group that charged against **20.1** for the group that did not, with the lag invariant at exactly 30.0 min across all 159 executed cases. The term window length hides is the **issue delay — 0.8 min vs 6.3 min**. And one row refutes the old predicate outright: a booking with a **15-minute window did charge**, which "window shorter than the lag cannot complete" calls impossible. **MECHANISM TWO was never counted anywhere in this repo: 22 of 183 (12%) had `begin_charge` REFUSED** — `target_occupied` **16**, `superseded` **6** — and the clock has nothing to say about any of them, since a refused command never executes and so has no lag to lose. **Refusal is not fatal:** 10 of the 22 charged regardless, so the lost turns are 8 + 4. **Still genuinely open:** the 2 sessions with no command, and whether mechanism two occurs at `operator_demo` granularity — unmeasured outside this run. **THE GRANULARITY DECISION IS TAKEN — [0312](db/checks/0312_three_decisions_taken_under_delegated_authority_and_the_cert_harness_is_fifteen_times_coarser_than_production.sql) §3: DO NOT re-granularise the nine canon columns.** Two of my own measurements were corrected first: `tick_interval_seconds` is **wall-clock pacing, not sim advance**, and reads **30 for every run in the database** including production runs advancing 2.00 and demo runs advancing 0.48 — reading it as the answer would have been the clock-domain error this repo has recorded nine times; and the canon's 6-tick columns do **not** run at 4 hours/tick, since **all 18 cert runs are 30.00 sim-min/tick** and the tick COUNT varies the SPAN. **That produces the real finding: `cert_harness` 30.00 sim-min/tick against `production_live` 2.00 — the certification harness is 15× coarser than the configuration that ships.** The nine columns stay because the property they prove is true at any tick size and both arms are equally coarse, so no verdict is wrong; re-granularising costs nine re-certifications and nine streaks for no new truth about determinism. **What was wrong was never the fixture — it was that the fixture's clock was invisible in the verdict**, since `ottoq_determinism_canon` and the verdict ledger record tick COUNT and have no tick-SIZE column at all. **[0402](db/migrations/0402_a_determinism_verdict_can_finally_state_the_clock_it_was_earned_at_g112.sql) fixes that and is APPLIED (`20260921165330`, `forces_recert` FALSE)**: `ottoq_determinism_verdict_clock` plus `ottoq_assert_verdict_clock_legibility()`. **Its first reading is the disclosure: 56 verdicts, 9 clock-recoverable, and coarsest = finest = 30.00** — not one recoverable verdict was earned at anything but 30 sim-min/tick, and 47 of 56 cannot state their clock at all because their arm runs were purged. Deliberately not done, and named: no column on `ottoq_determinism_canon` (**no function in the database writes that table**, so it would be a column nothing fills) and no edit to `ottoq_determinism_pair` (the certification path itself; stamping at write time is the durable fix and is the follow-up). **AND THIS UNBLOCKS G111 AND `0400`** — both were deferred on the argument that a granularity change would force a recert anyway; that free ride does not exist, so they batch on their own merits. |

So it **WARNS on a PR and FAILS on push to `main`**, where the two are supposed to agree. The script's own exit codes are respected rather than reinterpreted — notably **exit 2 = "could not compare" is a warning, never a green tick**, because the script was written to refuse a false pass — and an undocumented exit fails rather than being read charitably. Exercised directly rather than argued: the skip path emits `::notice::… This is a skip, not a pass.` and exits 0; a fake token reaches the script's own `REFUSING to report a pass without comparing. exit 2`; and the ref/exit matrix is main(0→0, **1→1**, 2→0, 7→1) against PR(0→0, 1→0, 2→0, 7→1). So the gate **can** fail and **cannot** fail for being a PR. **DECIDED 2026-09-21 AND THE STATUS CHANGES FROM PENDING TO CEILING — Chase declined the secret, and that is the right call read from the same source.** §7c asked for `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` while quoting Supabase's own line that a PAT *"carries your full account privileges"*; that sentence is the argument **against** giving one to CI, not a caveat attached to giving it. **There is no unauthenticated route to a deployed function's source, so NO CI configuration can compare repo against deployed** — the step will print its notice and exit 0 on every run from now on, and **a green `verify` never means `edge-functions/` matches deployment.** `verify.yml`'s comment and notice now say *declined by decision, not oversight*, so no future session re-asks. **THE SUBSTITUTE, run today ([0306](db/checks/0306_the_only_tests_that_cover_the_agent_chain_were_never_run_by_ci_and_had_been_red_for_two_days.sql) §8a):** the Supabase MCP connector reads deployed source in-session with **no repo secret** — **28 ACTIVE deployed against 28 `edge-functions/` directories, sets matching exactly**, and `ottoq-orchestrator-agent` **v30** carrying every marker the tests assert (the `skipped` branch, `engine: receipt.engine ?? null`, `solver_ran`, `assign`, the awaited `fetch(solverUrl`, and the G109 TEMPORAL comment in the deployed `_shared` copy), which **closes §3's four-hour production-only window.** **AND WHAT IT IS NOT: marker-level, not byte-level.** `_MANIFEST.md` disqualified `version` (77% false positives) and `updated_at` (2 FP + 1 FN of 28, the FN being the function whose drift held a live credential) by measurement, and refuses hand-transcription as a method — a tool result retyped from context IS transcription. `ezbr_sha256` does not help: it digests the bundled eszip, not the source. Only the declined token yields bytes. **THE RESIDUAL RISK IS HUMAN, and it is the same one that already fired:** an in-session check runs when someone remembers, and v29's four production-only hours were precisely a *when someone remembers* failure — so the substitute for the gate is the thing that failed once. **Standing instruction: any session that deploys an edge function re-reads the deployed source through the connector in that same session, because nothing downstream will.** G110 stays **open** as the one direction this repo has no automated guard for. |

| **G107** *(**CLOSED 2026-09-21 — diagnosed in [0297](db/checks/0297_the_kpi_diagnostic_was_right_and_the_world_is_wrong_every_demo_run_is_built_at_one_clock_and_ticked_from_another.sql), fixed by [0396](db/migrations/0396_choose_the_clock_before_you_build_the_world_g107.sql)** (applied `20260921025959`, `forces_recert` **TRUE**), **verified on published output in [0300](db/checks/0300_g107_closed_the_clock_fix_measured_on_published_output_and_the_number_that_was_negative_is_not.sql)**. It still owns `sim_clock_end`, which depends on `p_days`. **The clock keeps its exact value** — the old post-hoc expression is reproduced verbatim in the new place — **so `config_hash` is unchanged and only the world moves**, which is precisely why the classification is `forces_recert` **TRUE**: a recert floor comparing hashes alone would keep every column's streak while the run underneath it changed, and that is the G25/G28 refusal. **`ottoq_assert_dispatch_time_coherence(uuid)` ships with it, deliberately as a function and not a CHECK constraint** — the writer is the tick hot path and is unwrapped, so a blocking constraint would turn a data defect into a dead run, against this engine's own rule that one bad vehicle must never abort the telemetry step. **Proven to fire:** run against `c9b0a87e` it raises *"32 of 87 dispatches return before they were dispatched and 32 carry a negative `actual_duration_min` (worst inversion 439.5 min)"* — and the two witnesses agreeing at 32 each is independent confirmation that one UPDATE wrote both. **Certification verified unaffected:** three pairs (seeds 424242 and 171717, 12 and 24 ticks) ran *after* the migration applied and all read `validation_status='passed'`. **Still owed:** the five KPIs re-measured on a post-fix run, which `0297` said must come after the fix and not before. **PROVEN ON A LIVE RUN — [0299](db/checks/0299_the_verdict_ledger_0386_built_tonight_survived_the_exact_purge_it_was_built_for.sql) §3.** Run `71942fbf` (seed 100020, started 2026-09-21 04:01:56 UTC) carries `sim_clock_start` **2026-09-20 05:35:00+00 = 00:35 Central, the same value `c9b0a87e` had**, with `boot_prime.start_hour_cst` now **0** matching it and **`dispatched_in_the_future` = 0 where `c9b0a87e` had 45.** `0298` §1 showed that on a rolled-back probe; this is the first live run where it holds, so the clock did not move and the world is now built against it. **The five KPIs remain the outstanding deliverable** — the run needs its 540 sim-minute horizon, and they must be recorded before any subsequent demo run purges the `class='engine'` tables they are computed from, which is what `0395` exists for. **AND STARTING THAT RUN VALIDATED G93 BY ACCIDENT** ([0299](db/checks/0299_the_verdict_ledger_0386_built_tonight_survived_the_exact_purge_it_was_built_for.sql) §1): it purged all sixteen `cert_harness` rows from `ottoq_sim_runs`, and `ottoq_determinism_verdict_ledger` — added by `0386` earlier the same night because `ottoq_run_archives` held 1,166 determinism arms and zero verdicts — kept **9 verdicts, 9 passed, one `engine_hash`** straight through it. Everything hour-of-day shaped in a demo run — arrival curve, tariff windows, solar, staffing — is evaluated at a different hour than the world was built for. **AND IT IS INVISIBLE TO THE FOURTEEN ATOMS BY CONSTRUCTION:** `v_offset_min` is seed-derived, so both arms of every pair split their clocks identically, and a byte-identical verdict cannot see a defect both arms share. So 2.9a's apparatus stands; what falls is the demo runs, which are exactly the runs every KPI measured on 09-20 and 09-21 came from. **CLOSED 2026-09-21 06:43 UTC on published output — [0300](db/checks/0300_g107_closed_the_clock_fix_measured_on_published_output_and_the_number_that_was_negative_is_not.sql).** Run `71942fbf` completed at 1147 ticks / 550 sim-minutes, and the two tests `0297` nominated both pass. **`audit.hours_clipped_to_window` reads +0.52, where `c9b0a87e` read −227.36 and `e8b8eb3e` −237.29** — and that figure needs no baseline and no pairing to read, because a negative clip is arithmetically impossible from a coherent world. **`ottoq_assert_dispatch_time_coherence` returns `(101, 0, 0, −1.5, "coherent: all 101 dispatches return after they were dispatched")`**, against the 32-of-87 it raised on `c9b0a87e`; `dispatched_in_the_future` 0 and `negative_durations` 0 confirm it independently. `provenance.not_reproducible` is empty and `horizon_source` is `run_horizon`, so all eight metrics are reproducible from the run ID. **The other KPIs moved and `0300` §3b refuses to claim why**: `config_hash` (1f6a3be3 vs 89e34c73), `engine_hash` (cf3622a0 vs ec8dfa03) and the run length all changed between the two runs, so that table is a trend log, not a controlled pair — a controlled answer needs `ottoq_determinism_pair`-style pairing on one `engine_hash`, which is `0145`/`0146`'s gap. **And `0300` §4 keeps one thing open that the fix does not touch:** `returns_unserved` 12 with `p95` 147.7 against a `p50` of 1.5 is a 98x median-to-tail spread, which is `0250`/`0264`'s staging-and-charging capacity question and must not be reported as a clock defect now that the clock is fixed. |

| **G106** *(**MEASURED; ONE DIAGNOSIS ATTEMPTED AND RETRACTED** — [0296](db/checks/0296_g102_engaged_and_one_run_gave_a_cleaner_comparison_than_the_pair_i_was_building.sql) §3 posed the readings, §5 chose a third one from a code read and then killed it with the `SELECT` it had skipped, §6 has the finished-run figures.)* | **The engine's dominant remaining proposer churn carries a reason the proposer's own author flagged as "something unexpected" — 122 of 127 abstentions on run `c9b0a87e`, from FOUR vehicles, at 30.5 each.** The string is *"no charge operation in the returned plan"*. Read at source (`proposer/forward_proposer.py` ~line 1010), it is the **second** branch of a two-way choice: `served is False` → *"the site could not serve this vehicle within its capacity"*; `served` **absent** → this one, under the comment *"rejection was off, so no charge op means something unexpected; say that rather than inventing a reason."* **So this is not the normal capacity decline** — that reads differently, arrives with `served: False`, and does not appear on this run at all. | `0296` §3: the abstention census on `c9b0a87e` grouped by `rationale.abstained_by` and `reason`, plus the branch read from `forward_proposer.py` | **Two readings, and `0296` deliberately does not choose between them:** (a) a vehicle is reaching the solve that should not have — the serviceable filter admits something at or above its target SoC, so the model correctly gives it no charge op, making this a **filter defect in the proposer**; or (b) the plan's shape carries assets the charge set does not cover, making it a **contract mismatch** between the solver's output and the translator's assumption. **Which one is a code read, not a measurement.** **AND THE FIX SHAPE DIFFERS FROM G102's, which is the point for seam 3.** A `not_due` abstention carries `planned_start_min` — a TIME at which the answer changes — which is exactly why suppression works on it.
