that is G62's `agent_calls_with_no_l1_rules = 1,120 of 1,120` reproduced on a fresh seed and localised to one context. `itinerary_amended` (30, `twin.ottoq_sim_advance_flow_contract`) and `triage_verdict` (16, `twin.ottoq_sim_advance_visit_atoms`) are the **twin advancing its own world** — shielding them would ask the rules' permission for something the world already did. `bay_reconcile` (5, `ottoq.ottoq_bind_unbooked_bay_occupants`) binds a vehicle **already physically in a bay** — the `space_conflict_ledger` posture rule 6 requires, so gating it would mean refusing to write down a fact. **`gate_intake_no_charge` (12, `public.ottoq_decide_tick`) IS a gap:** its `(3b) GATE INTAKE — NO-CHARGE ARRIVALS` loop calls `ottoq.ottoq_book_stall` for a staging stall, stamps `to_stall_id` on the itinerary leg, writes `'enacted'`, and emits `proceed_to_stall` — **a stall claimed and a vehicle commanded to drive to it, with no `ottoq_shield_probe` anywhere between the loop's `FOR` and its `END LOOP`.** `0263` §1 came within one step of this by finding that four of the five codes at `stall_assignment` are charge-specific and cannot judge a parking hold. **AND `l2_engine` cannot be used to find these:** `trg_ottoq_stamp_l2_engine` sets `COALESCE(NULLIF(enacted_action->>'source',''), l2_engine, 'deterministic_v1')`, and on all 42 unshielded `task_start` rows `enacted_action->>'source'` is NULL — so their `deterministic_v1` label is the trigger's **fallback**, and *"deterministic_v1 did it"* is indistinguishable from *"nobody stamped it"*. Same defect shape as G99. | **open — instrument LANDED, remedy waiting.** `0392` adds `public.ottoq_enactment_branches` (a declared vocabulary for `resolved_action_context`: writer, side of the sim boundary, and whether a shield is **expected**), plus `ottoq_shield_coverage(run)` and `ottoq_assert_shield_coverage(run)`. Declared rather than hardcoded for the reason `ottoq_kpi_touch_actor_types` declares which actors are human — the per-branch judgement is what a reader must be able to audit. **The number to act on is `unshielded_gap`** (unshielded AND either declared `shield_expected` or not declared at all); `unshielded_by_design` is the remainder and is not a defect. P4 asserts both sum identities, the check `0341` had to add to `ottoq_intelligence_ledger` after its first version bucketed 1,161 rows nowhere. **The underlying deficiency this exposes: `ottoq_decisions` does not record which function wrote it** — twelve insert sites in `ottoq_decide_tick` alone, eight database functions plus one edge function, with free text as the only proxy. **Remedy, both halves `forces_recert TRUE` and both deferred until run `e8b8eb3e` completes so the 0390 measurement is not perturbed:** (1) route the agent through `ottoq_external_proposals` as `llm_advisor` — which `ottoq_proposer_precedence` already declares at **rank 20, `holds_tick=true`, `greedy_yields=true`**, and which has submitted **0 proposals ever** (0 live, 0 of 6,681 evidence-ledger rows) — closing the ungated path AND G62's latency defect at once, because a proposal is inherently asynchronous; (2) add the probe to the gate-intake loop. **Not claimed:** that any unshielded enactment has actually violated a rule — no rule was evaluated, so nothing is known either way, and that is the whole finding. **CORRECTED 2026-09-20 BY [0292](db/checks/0292_g100s_agent_claim_is_wrong_the_dials_are_gated_at_policy_write_and_the_gap_is_attribution.sql), FIXED BY [0393](db/migrations/0393_the_branch_i_led_0392_with_is_gated_at_a_probe_point_i_did_not_look_for.sql) — THE `orchestrator_agent` HALF OF THIS ROW WAS WRONG, IN THE DIRECTION RULE 6 CALLS MORE DANGEROUS, AND IT WAS A SAFETY CLAIM.** I wrote that the agent is *"the one path on which an AI changes engine state and the only path with no L1 evaluation"*. **The agent's dials ARE L1-gated.** The chain, four hops, each one query: (1) the edge function writes ONE **depot-scoped** `ottoq_decisions` row per tick whose whole effect set is dial writes and ops actions; (2) `public.ottoq_apply_ops_action`'s every whitelisted branch is a `ottoq_policy_set` call **and nothing else**, each clamped in its own branch, with anything off-whitelist INSERTed into `ottoq_ops_approvals` as `pending` — the human queue — so **the agent's entire physical reach is policy dials: no stall, no vehicle, no booking**; (3) `public.ottoq_policy_set` calls `ottoq_shield_probe` with `policy_write`; (4) **254 `ottoq_rule_evaluations` rows of `AI.001.agent_dial_within_envelope` at `policy_write`/`policy_param` on run `e8b8eb3e` — the very run 0392 measured.** So the edge function's own header (*"L1 shield still gates every physical effect; vehicle-first inviolable"*) is **accurate**, and I contradicted it from the data without reading the path it describes. **What is real is an ATTRIBUTION gap, and it is much smaller:** the evaluation happens at a different `action_context`, on a different `entity_type`, and **nothing joins the two** — so `agent_calls_with_no_l1_rules = 1,120 of 1,120` (CLAUDE.md rule 6, from 0340) is a **correctly computed metric with a misleading name**: it measures *"the agent's row does not carry its own rule results"* and reads as *"the agent is ungated"*. Third instance of the `offerable` shape in two days, and this time the misreader was me. **AND THE REMEDY CHANGES SHAPE, which is why this is a check file and not a quiet edit:** routing the agent through `ottoq_shield_and_log` — 0392's proposal — would have **closed the finding on paper and changed nothing**, because that function hardcodes `entity_type='vehicle'` and `CONTINUE`s on any action with no `vehicle_id`, so it would skip every agent row it was given. What is needed is a **`correlation_id`** (the column already exists on `ottoq_rule_evaluations`) threaded from the agent's decision into `ottoq_policy_set`'s probe call. Additive, and it changes nothing about what the shield decides. **WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS STILL WORTH HAVING:** the rules-side blind spot is untouched (§3 of 0292 is itself a rules-side count reporting 23 of 30, while `ottoq_assert_shield_coverage` reports 170 unshielded enacted decisions on the same run — both right, different questions, and only one was being asked before 0392); the instrument stands and **is already earning its keep** (a previously unseen undeclared unshielded branch, `reservation_reopt`, surfaced as the run advanced); and **`gate_intake_no_charge` is still a real gap and is now the only declared one** — it is vehicle-scoped, so `ottoq_shield_and_log` fits it exactly, and unlike the agent there is no second probe point downstream because a staging booking is not a policy write. `0393` sets `orchestrator_agent` to `shield_expected=false` with the reason and the probe point recorded, and its preflight **asserts all four hops** rather than trusting the note — because getting this wrong in either direction is a safety claim. **AND 0292 §3 RETIRES A SECOND STALE SENTENCE, ALSO IN OUR FAVOUR:** CLAUDE.md 2.5 says *"twenty-one of thirty declared rules, at six decision points"*. Measured on this run: **23 of 30, at EIGHT probe points** — the new two are `vehicle_state_change` (628 evaluations, SM.001) and `stall_state_change` (581, SM.003), both of which `0273` listed among its nine unevaluated codes and both of which **`db/migrations/0387` wired hours after `0273` was written**. `0273`'s caveat survives and still governs: evaluated is a WIRING count, not a PROTECTION count, so 23 of 30 is the optimistic bound. Seven remain unevaluated, four `critical` — HW.006, SM.004, SM.005 (G97's population), SM.006 (G96), plus SLA.002, TW.002, TW.004. **The lesson, stated so it generalises:** 0392 was built on `resolved_action_context` — a field that says what a decision was ABOUT — and I read it as though it said what had been CHECKED. **An audit instrument is not evidence about the thing it is pointed at until the path it measures has been read end to end**, and 0392 shipped with four of five branches read that way and the fifth, the one I led with, not read at all. |

For a charge stall it is right. **The defect is in the READER, and tonight the reader was me.** `proposer/forward_proposer.py` is unaffected — it consults `offerable` only for charge-capable stalls, the population the key describes. **The rule this earns, which generalises past this field:** a boolean named for an OUTCOME (*offerable*) rather than its DOMAIN (*charge-stall offerable*) will be read outside its domain, and the reading will look like a measurement. CLAUDE.md's three-gate rule says to name which gate refused; this adds that you must first check the predicate is **defined for the population you are counting**. | **open.** **An open question it leaves, stated as a question:** **792 `twin.staging_overflow` events** fired on this run (422 night tail, 370 wave, continuous 00:42-09:37 CT, against a prior run's 258) while at least 40 staging stalls were provably free on both gates. **I am NOT claiming `offerable` causes the overflow** — the producer is a twin event and may read the pointers or calendar directly, never the frame; establishing the link needs it read end to end and that is the next measurement. What IS established: 792 overflow events and ~40 free staging stalls coexist, so at least one of the two is not measuring what its name says, and **`0288` §3's capacity conclusion must not be extended into a claim about STAGING capacity until that is resolved.** **Three remedies, none small:** (a) rename to `charge_offerable` — honest, and `forward_proposer.py` pins `OFFERABLE_KEY` while the frame is digested into `frame_hash`, so `forces_recert TRUE` invalidating all nine canon columns; (b) add a second fact for non-charge stalls (pointer AND calendar, no charger term) — additive, same recert cost, and it gives future readers the thing they will otherwise fabricate; (c) leave it and document, which is what 0289 does. **(b) is what I would build**, gated on someone actually needing staging availability in the frame — today no consumer does, which is precisely why the field has been wrong for 118 stalls without anything breaking. |
| **G98** *(**MEASURED, NOT FIXED** — [0288](db/checks/0288_cp_sat_was_starved_not_weak_and_feeding_it_did_not_give_it_influence.sql), **TWO NUMBERS CORRECTED BY [0290](db/checks/0290_g98s_two_percent_was_the_wrong_denominator_and_the_churn_is_the_real_defect.sql) — see the end of this row; the direction survives, the arithmetic did not.** The next measurement is named in the Fix column; no migration accompanies this.)* | **CP-SAT was STARVED, not weak — and feeding it did not give it influence. 12 of 610 proposals enacted, 2.0%.** Within ONE run (`c8f678fb`, busy_day, seed **100020** chosen so its 540-sim-minute window spans 00:35-09:37 CT and therefore contains both the night tail AND the morning return wave): **night tail 258 fires, 8 planning (3.1%), 14 vehicles planned; morning wave 106 fires, 68 planning (64.2%), 383 vehicles planned** — 27x the planning volume on 41% as many fires, with `arrived_at_gate` going 0 -> 5 and `staged_awaiting_service` 1 -> 19. Same seed, same engine, same descriptor logic: the only difference is how many vehicles wanted a stall. So `0282` §3's night figures were a TROUGH, not a ceiling. **But the bottleneck moved rather than cleared:** of 610 `forward_lex` proposals across 56 vehicles (**10.89 per vehicle**), **539 (88.4%) superseded `entity_decided_by_other_proposal`**, 20 superseded `newer_proposal_same_entity`, 20 `proposer_abstained`, 16 `stall_reserved`, **12 enacted (2.0%)**. | `db/checks/0288` §1-2; the fire log split on the snapshot clock at 11:00 UTC, and the disposition census joined to `ottoq_decisions` | **WHO SUPERSEDES THEM IS SETTLED, NOT GUESSED.** All other external proposers put **9 rows total** on this run (`greedy_constrained` 4, `ottoq_service_priority` 5), so they cannot account for 539. This is `0184`'s tie-break finding (*"a forward_lex row is heard but LOSES the tie-break to the local path's regenerated row by created_at"*) and `0255`'s supersession dominance, both at volume for the first time. **10.89 proposals per vehicle is the same mechanism's second half:** a superseded proposal is no longer live, so `vehicle_is_held` admits the vehicle again and the next fire re-plans it — G60/`0292`'s churn, except the overwriting is now mostly done TO the proposer rather than BY it. **AND THE CAPACITY WALL APPEARS HERE FOR THE FIRST TIME**, which is what rule 8's depth goal actually asks for: `p95_time_to_service` **39.6 min** against the night run's **2.7**, and `returns_unserved` **11** against **0**, while turns/point/day rises 2.48 -> 3.56 and asset-hours fall 102.51 -> 57.89. The depot works harder and loses ground — and this only becomes visible when the arrival wave exists, which is why every prior measurement of this depot looked comfortable. **G97 also reproduces on this second seed**: touch_events 610 = operator 78 + override 532, so 87% is the shield again and the human figure is 0.140. | **open — AND THE NEXT MEASUREMENT IS NOW DONE (`0288` §6): IT IS A WIRING GAP, AND THE REMEDY IS A DESIGN DECISION.** Hold coverage PER PROPOSAL, the denominator §5 refused to guess at: of 559 superseded proposals only **14 (2.5%)** had a deferral spanning that vehicle at that tick, though **414 (74.1%)** were for vehicles held at SOME tick. So the hold is armed for three quarters of these vehicles and almost never when the proposal was made. **The cause is one query:** neither door arms a hold — `ottoq_proposer_submit_batch` and `ottoq_submit_external_proposal` both mention `first_refusal` NO and write `ottoq_cuopt_deferrals` NO; only `ottoq_cuopt_first_refusal_arm`, called from `ottoq_decide_tick`, does. **The right-of-first-refusal is armed by the DECIDER, not by the DOOR, so submitting a proposal buys no protection.** That is coherent rather than careless: the mechanism's name says `cuopt`, and the cuOpt path runs the other way round — the decide tick initiates the request, so the decider arms the hold BEFORE dispatching and the proposal returns into a seat already reserved. The CP-SAT bridge inverts it: an external process pushes a proposal in at an arbitrary moment between ticks, gets no seat, and by the next tick the local path has decided the entity. That is the 96.2% mechanically. **Three remedies with different meanings:** (a) arm at the door — strongest, and lets any external proposer stall the kernel; (b) arm at the tick boundary for whatever is pending — keeps arming with the decider, one extra read per tick, cannot be abused by submission timing; (c) leave it and accept CP-SAT as advisory — honest, and then **2% enactment is the designed outcome and every claim about CP-SAT's contribution must be quoted at 2%**. **(b) is what I would build** and it is NOT built: arming from the door changes when the deterministic path is required to defer, on the tick path, affecting throughput — the class CLAUDE.md 2.5 reserves and rule 6 puts on the product side. Chase's call. **I explicitly refused to claim the hold is broken.** `proposer_hold_enabled=1` was armed (7 of 7) and the machinery IS firing — `ottoq_cuopt_deferrals` recorded **63** holds, every one spent AND cleared, across 63 vehicles and ticks 1-1203. 63 against ~610 proposals LOOKS like a tenth of coverage, but a hold may be armed once per vehicle-VISIT rather than once per proposal and I do not have that denominator. **A ratio without its denominator is the unit error `0284` §3 and `0285` §1 both record; three times in one night is enough.** **CORRECTED 2026-09-20 by `0290`, and in the direction that UNDERSTATED our own solver, which rule 6 forbids exactly as firmly as the flattering direction.** **(i) "12 of 610 proposals enacted, 2.0%" is the wrong denominator.** 610 is proposals SUBMITTED, and a vehicle takes at most one enacted assignment however many times it is proposed for. The run made **240** enacted `stall_assignment` decisions across 98 vehicles: **deterministic_v1 130 (54.2%, 77 veh)**, reservation_honoured 47 (19.6%), greedy_constrained 22 (9.2%), cuopt 15 (6.3%), **forward_lex 12 (5.0%, 12 veh)**, reservation_reopt 12 (5.0%), reservation_reassigned 2. **The influence figure is 5.0% of enacted assignments, not 2.0% of anything.** **(ii) "96.2% superseded by the local decide path" was a tautology wearing a percentage** — that query read `ottoq_decisions` with `action_context='stall_assignment'` WITHOUT filtering on source, and that table records every enacted assignment whatever proposed it, including forward_lex's own 12 and cuopt's 15. It means *"the vehicle got decided by someone"*. The defensible version is the table above: `deterministic_v1` is the single largest winner at 54.2%. G98's direction was right; its evidence was not. **(iii) THE HOLD REMEDY IS WITHDRAWN.** `ottoq_cuopt_first_refusal_arm` explicitly EXCLUDES any vehicle that already has a pending `holds_tick` proposal (*"cuOpt has already answered for this vehicle => let the cursor enact it now"*), so the hold is a PRE-proposal seat reservation and 2.5% coverage is the DESIGNED behaviour, not a gap — option (b) would have inverted the function's own intent. The hold is working. **(iv) THE REAL DEFECT IS CHURN: 610 proposals, 56 vehicles, 12 enactments — 50.8 proposals per enactment.** `vehicle_is_held` withholds a vehicle only while it carries a LIVE PENDING proposal, and `ottoq_dispose_external_proposals` runs every tick, so a superseded proposal makes its vehicle admissible again and the next fire re-plans it against a frame that has barely moved. **A churning proposer slanders itself:** fire once per vehicle-decision and the same 12 enactments would read ~21% instead of 2%. **Act in this order — (1) cut the churn (proposer-side, no tick-path change, no recert); (2) THEN ask whether `deterministic_v1` should yield to a pending rank-0 proposal the way `ottoq_l2_optimize_assignments` already yields via `greedy_yields`** — symmetric completion of an existing mechanism, but a tick-path change, `forces_recert TRUE`, and uninterpretable until the instrument is fixed. **Not claimed:** that CP-SAT would fix the capacity wall or that the local path causes it. Pricing the difference needs C5's CRN-paired A/B with the shield held constant (`0146`), which does not exist yet. One run, one seed, one wave. |

dwelling, so either the matrix is wrong and 3,426 findings evaporate, or the dwell is real and the twin has modelled a duty cycle the hardware would refuse, which every `bess_setpoint_kw` command and the whole `energy_mpc_follow` path inherits. **The G94 shape: two readings, opposite remedies, measurement cannot choose.** (iii) the probe belongs on the `bess_snapshots` INSERT comparing to the previous row per `system_id` — 91k rows at this depot, so its cost needs measuring before it is added. **Promoting SM.006 to `block` today would refuse 14.4% of the battery's transitions**, which is why 2.9a measures before enforcing. **DECIDED 2026-09-20 — [0295](db/checks/0295_sm006s_dwell_is_not_a_grid_requirement_and_our_thirty_second_tick_cannot_observe_it_anyway.sql), the one item tonight that needed an EXTERNAL source.** **Do NOT wire SM.006 as written.** Its parenthetical — *"dwell-through-standby between charge and discharge"* — is **not a grid requirement**, and the 14.4% of transitions it flags are **not evidence of anything**. **Sourced, with its confidence labelled:** IEEE 1547-2018 requires a DER mode transition to complete in **no greater than 30 seconds**, smooth over 5–300 s, and its DER definition explicitly includes exporting energy storage — [NREL fy20osti/75436](https://docs.nrel.gov/docs/fy20osti/75436.pdf), [IREC](https://irecusa.org/blog/regulatory-engagement/how-ieee-1547-1-2020-paves-the-way-for-more-energy-storage-a-smarter-grid/), [Sandia 2023](https://www.sandia.gov/app/uploads/sites/273/2023/11/2023_Vermont_Webinar_Vartanian1.pdf), searched 2026-09-20. **That is a CEILING on transition duration, not a FLOOR requiring a rest in standby**, and nothing found imposes a minimum dwell or requires passing through standby. **I did not read IEEE 1547-2018 itself** — paywalled, and the university-hosted copy that surfaced was deliberately not treated as authoritative — so this is strong indicative evidence, not a settled vendor fact, and `docs/research/requests/R-19` asks Hermes the four narrow questions that would make it primary. **AND THE SHARPER FINDING IS ABOUT OUR CLOCK, not about inverters.** The twin's tick is **30 seconds** — *the same as IEEE 1547's transition ceiling*. So a fully compliant inverter may begin charging and be discharging by the next sample with the whole transition, dwell or not, invisible between two ticks. **A consecutive-snapshot `charge` → `discharge` pair is therefore not evidence that no dwell occurred**: the 3,426 flagged transitions are consistent with perfect compliance and with none, and nothing at this resolution distinguishes them. That is the `0289` predicate/observation mismatch a fourth time, from the other direction — there a field answered a narrower question than it was read as answering; here the DATA answers a coarser question than the rule asks. **So wiring it would have been worse than leaving it unwired:** a `critical`/`block` rule at `bess_state_change` would have refused 14.4% of BESS transitions on the grounds of a dwell it cannot see, enforcing a requirement no source supports. And `0292` §3's note that SM.006's *"input does exist"* is **retracted** — what exists is a sample too coarse to carry the question. **The decision: split it.** Keep the transition-matrix half (a real state-machine invariant, observable at any resolution, exactly what `0387` wired successfully for SM.001/SM.003) and **delete the dwell clause** from the `critical` rule — or move it to a separate, non-blocking rule whose rationale cites battery wear rather than grid legality and which stays unenforced until the twin samples finely enough to see a transition. A rule asserting a grid requirement nothing establishes is the `0231` defect in rule form. **STEPS 0 AND 1 DONE — [0397](db/migrations/0397_one_word_for_rest_and_the_dwell_that_lived_in_two_missing_rows_g96.sql), evidence in [0298](db/checks/0298_the_clock_fix_verified_and_sm006_cannot_be_wired_yet_because_the_bess_has_two_words_for_rest.sql) §2. Going to wire it, three things turned up and each changed the plan.** **(a)** `ottoq_eval_sm_006_bess_transition` contains **no dwell logic at all** — it delegates to `ottoq_eval_sm_transition_validity`, which looks up `(entity_kind, from_state, to_state)` in `ottoq_state_transitions` and checks `allowed_actor_types`. No timing, no clock. **(b) But the dwell IS enforced, as a MATRIX OMISSION** — of 17 active `bess` transitions, `charging → discharging` and `discharging → charging` number **ZERO**. So the dwell was never prose or a timer: it was **two rows nobody inserted**, and dropping it means *inserting* them, which `0397` does. **(c) And the real blocker was a WORD, not the dwell.** `twin.ottoq_sim_bess_step` wrote `ottoq_bess_units.current_state = 'idle'` and `bess_snapshots.status = 'standby'` **from the same `v_actual_kw` in the same statement pair**, with a comment conceding it (*"'idle' is the unit-level vocabulary"*). The `bess_status` enum and the matrix both use `standby` and **neither knows `idle`**; the column is plain `text` with no CHECK, which is why the drift was writable, and all 3 live units read `idle`. **The trap:** a `bess_state_change` probe naturally reads the unit column, and passing `idle` refuses every transition into or out of rest — a `critical`/`block` rule taking the BESS offline on the first tick. Not 14.4%; nearly all of them. `0397` unifies on `standby` across the censused four sites (three writers, plus the one reader `ottoq_trigger_emergency_cascade` made tolerant of both because pre-0397 rows say `idle`), rewrites the 3 live rows, inserts the two transitions, supersedes SM.006 to **v2** with the parenthetical gone and the unsourced *"physically impossible for the inverter/contactor"* rationale replaced by `0295`'s IEEE 1547-2018 finding, and adds `ottoq_assert_bess_state_vocabulary()`. **`forces_recert TRUE`, measured not assumed:** `ottoq.ottoq_world_fingerprint` hashes `COALESCE(b.current_state,'-')` in its BESS section. **STEP 2, wiring the probe, is deliberately still open:** a `critical`/`block` gate must not depend on a vocabulary change that has not yet survived a run — `0294` §2's lesson. **STEP 2 IS DONE AND G96 IS CLOSED 2026-09-21 12:28 UTC — [0399](db/migrations/0399_the_bess_state_machine_gets_its_caller_and_the_table_gets_its_first_trigger_g96.sql) (applied `20260921122812`), evidence in [0307](db/checks/0307_sm006_has_a_caller_and_the_first_thing_it_found_is_a_writer_the_snapshot_history_cannot_see.sql).** The vocabulary change survived two runs (`71942fbf`, `f13fc580`) and `ottoq_assert_bess_state_vocabulary()` reads *reconciled*, so the `0294` §2 condition above is met. `ottoq_bess_units` gets **the first trigger it has ever had** — `AFTER UPDATE OF current_state` **with a WHEN clause**, so the function body is never entered for the ~197k of **225,304** lifetime updates that move only SoC on **3** live rows, and the evaluator's `'no-op transition'` pass is unreachable by construction (the G28 trap). A trigger rather than a probe inside a writer because **`UPDATE ... ottoq_bess_units` appears in TEN functions** — one probe would cover one and silently exempt nine, which is `0292`'s mistake. **Before-measurement, as `0387` §5 demanded:** all **28,735** transitions this depot's battery has ever made fall in six shapes and SM.006 v2 passes **6 of 6** under actor `ottoq_engine` — so it refuses nothing, where it would have refused **4,032 (14.0%)** before `0397` inserted the two reversal rows and **all 28,735** on the role gate without the attribution. Five preflight assertions, each testing something that was false within the preceding eighteen hours. `forces_recert` **TRUE** on a narrow reason — a NEW WRITER into the rules atom, one of the fourteen, must be proven to write identically in both arms — and the sweep it started was **passing as it went, 3 of 9 re-certified within three minutes**. **G44 moves to 24 of 30 at NINE probe points**, with `0273`'s caveat travelling unchanged: evaluated is a WIRING count, not a protection count, and SM.006 binds nothing because it is MEASURE-ONLY by design. **What the probe found in those three minutes is G111**, and it is the reason promotion stays a separate decision. |
| **G89** *(**NOW INSTRUMENTED — `0374`/`0375`, `db/checks/0269`. Still NOT enforced**, and enforcement remains an energy-policy decision because a site-sum rule changes what the BESS is allowed to do, and so changes `ottoq-energy-mpc`'s setpoints and the `energy_mpc_follow` path. `db/checks/0265`, `0269`.)* **UPDATE 2026-09-20 12:45 UTC — the finding holds, the mechanism is sharper, and one of its numbers wanted context.** (1) **Nothing needed reconstructing: `grid_import_kw` IS the site total**, equal to the component sum in **8,050 of 8,050** snapshots on this depot at a worst residual of **0.10 kW**. (2) **The mechanism is an incomplete meter, not merely an unchecked sum.** EN.001 *does* compare against `service_max_kw`, but its load term is `ottoq_depot_current_demand_kw` → `twin.ottoq_sim_compute_charger_load_kw` = `SUM(ocpp_sessions power)` **and nothing else** — so it asks a true question, correctly answered, about a quantity that is not the site's load. (3) **The rate is 1 in 8,050 snapshots across 9 runs, and the distribution is why that is not comfort:** buckets covering **1,800–2,700 kW are empty** — the site has never operated between 1,714 and 2,739 kW, so it does not ride the cap and tip over, it takes one ~1,000 kW step (the BESS charge magnitude) clean over. The rarity is the rarity of the *coincidence*; nothing prevents it. **What `0374` installs:** `ottoq_site_power_excursion_ledger` (`class='evidence'`, append-only, no FK) plus a per-tick detector above the policy branch, and `ottoq_site_power_ledger` as the reader — so "how often has this site exceeded its service contract" is answerable across purges for the first time. **THE LIMITATION, STATED PLAINLY:** G89's own recommendation was a projected-sum check *at `bess_dispatch`*, i.e. **before** the decision. What was built is a **meter, not a projection** — it witnesses the excursion after the fact and cannot prevent or predict one. That is deliberate (it catches an excursion however it arises, not only via a BESS decision, and it is what makes the historical rate answerable) but it does **not** discharge the pre-decision check, which remains unbuilt. **And `0375` corrects `0374`:** `0374` shipped `usual_dominant_component` claiming to answer "what pushed us over"; it answers "what is the biggest load", and the two disagree here — it reads `ev_charging` (1,691.8) while deferring the 968 kW BESS charge alone brings 2,738.8 under the cap with **729 kW to spare**, so a reader following it would throttle vehicles for nothing. Renamed `largest_component`, joined by `excursions_bess_deferral_clears` / `excursions_needing_ev_action`, which read **1 / 1 / 0** — *every excursion on record would have been cleared by deferring the battery, and none required refusing a vehicle.* **CORRECTION 2026-09-20 13:45 UTC (`db/checks/0271`, captured by `0378`) — G89'S SENTENCE "NOTHING ASSERTS THE SUM" IS FALSE, AND I REPEATED IT IN `0374`'S HEADER, ITS TABLE COMMENT, ITS REGISTRY NOTE AND ITS CERT-LINEAGE NOTE.** I went to build the projected-sum check G89 asked for and rule 5 stopped me: **it already exists.** `ottoq_energy_orchestrate` computes `v_charge_cap := GREATEST(50, v_demand_target − v_base_load + v_solar + v_bess_dispatch)` every tick — the battery's own draw subtracted kW for kW, which is the cumulative reasoning G89 said was absent — and publishes it as a `charge_cap_kw` command. **What survives is narrower and still real: no RULE asserts it.** The orchestrator publishes, it does not refuse, and **all 1,260** of the run's caps carry `"advisory": true` in their own payload. Against what actually flowed the advisory cap is mostly respected: **26 of 1,260 snapshots (2.1%) drew more EV load than the cap in force, worst overshoot 205.0 kW.** **THE EXCURSION HAD A SPECIFIC CAUSE, AND IT IS A PLANNING DEFECT, NOT A MISSING CONSTRAINT.** `v_demand_target` starts as `service_max_kw × factor` (2,500 × **0.9** = 2,250 — the policy value, not the 0.50 default in the source; I computed a first version against 1,250 and had to redo it), then is **replaced outright** by `ottoq_bess_reserve_target(...)` with **no `LEAST(v_service_max, …)`**. That function binary-searches for the lowest grid-import ceiling the battery can hold and **returns the forecast peak when it can hold nothing** — a *capability* answer used as a *permission*. At 13.81% SoC it returned **2,802** against a 2,500 contract, giving a **1,762 kW** cap where the contract-derived arithmetic (2,250 − 72 + 0 − 968) gives **1,210**. Measured: **16 of 1,260 targets exceeded `service_max_kw`**, worst **2,959** (459 over), worst published cap **2,804.4** — itself above the contract — while the **median target was 1,048**, so the override normally tightens by more than half and is a good mechanism that fails only when the battery is empty and demand is high. **THE FIX IS ONE LINE AND IS NOT MINE:** `LEAST(v_service_max, COALESCE(ottoq_bess_reserve_target(...), v_demand_target))`. It lowers the EV charge cap in exactly those 16 ticks — trading charging throughput at the worst moment for staying inside the contract. Right on the evidence, but a throughput call on the one depot whose capacity is the open question (rule 8). **`0378` captures the 16 rows into `class='evidence'` before the next demo run deletes `ottoq_energy_commands` and with it every number above.** **AND IT WEAKENS A cuOpt CLAIM I MADE TWICE:** `0374` and `db/checks/0269` both call this "the first measured instance of the cumulative-resource construct cuOpt cannot express." Overreach — a cumulative site-power resource *is* reasoned about here, in SQL, per tick. Truer: **the site power cap is currently held by an advisory publication plus an unclamped heuristic, where a scheduler with a first-class cumulative resource would carry it as a hard constraint.** Still a point for CP-SAT, a weaker and more honest one. **AND THE STRONG VERSION OF THAT CLAIM WAS ALREADY TRUE ELSEWHERE — `db/checks/0274`.** It needs no excursion at all: every one of the four constructs R-12 places outside cuOpt 26.08 is modelled in `solvers/cpsat/` **with a test that can fail** — **T3b** proves the site power cap BINDS (free peak 550 kW → 320 kW under a 330 kW cap), **T2** holds point exclusivity *and* the 18-minute DCFC cooldown on the service point, **T5** holds concurrency-within-point plus moves as scheduled operations. Plus 2.5's four determinism pins, all present and asserted in CI: `ortools==` pinned in `requirements.txt` alone (9.15.6755), `max_deterministic_time` always set with the wall clock only on explicit request and then stamped `reproducible: false`, `num_search_workers = 1`, and a canary that is **byte-identical across two solves, across a fresh process, and on a truncated budget against five contending cores**. So the sentence to use is about our own instrument rather than a competitor's limits: *"every construct the vendor documentation places outside cuOpt is modelled in our CP-SAT prototype, each covered by a test that can fail, and the plan is byte-identical across solves, across processes, and under CPU contention on a truncated budget."* | **Two independently legal decisions summed to 239 kW over the depot's declared service maximum, and no rule asserts the sum.** At the highest-import snapshot of run `5b37ee46`: EV charging **1,691.8 kW** (under the 1,800 kW `dcfc_max_concurrent_kw` nameplate), BESS **−968.0 kW** (*charging*, drawing from the grid), solar 0.0, building+lighting 79.0 — **grid import 2,738.8 kW against `service_max_kw` 2,500, a 9.6% overshoot, and the decomposition reconstructs the import to 0.1 kW.** Neither decision broke its own limit; their sum was never checked. **`bess_dispatch` evaluates exactly ONE rule** — `EN.003.bess_limits`, 234 evaluations, 0 failures — which checks the battery's own envelope; `EN.001.grid_capacity_ceiling` caps *aggregate charging* at `stall_assignment` and `charge_session_start`. **Nothing asserts `charging + BESS_charging + base_load ≤ service_max_kw`.** The shield gates each consumer against its own limit and never against the shared one. It happened **eight minutes into the run** (sim 06:56:09 against a 06:48 start) — the cold-start surge — and the second and third highest imports, within seconds, carry `bess_output_kw = 0`, so the battery draw is what took this one over rather than the charging wave alone. | `db/checks/0265` §1–2: the depot's declared caps, 1 of 1,139 snapshots over `service_max_kw`, 0 of 1,139 over the charging nameplate, deepest BESS charge −989.4 kW, and the per-component reconstruction | **Deliberately not fixed.** The cheap interim if one is wanted is a **MEASURED-only** rule at `bess_dispatch` logging the projected sum without blocking — 2.9a's blind-spot promotion doctrine, the shape 0370 used. | **open, and it is the most useful finding of the night for the CP-SAT decision.** This is **2.3's "shared site power cap"**, which 2.3 names as one of the four load-bearing constructs of the domain model and which 2.5 — citing `docs/research/answers/R-12` — records that **cuOpt cannot express at all**, as one of the four reasons the CP-SAT decomposition is *forced rather than chosen*. So it is the first measured instance on the twin depot of the exact constraint class the solver argument rests on, and the sharpest available answer to what the always-on CP-SAT host actually buys: **a cumulative site-power constraint that a per-consumer rule structurally cannot hold, because a per-consumer rule cannot see a sum.** |

The **observers** both say yes: `ottoq.ottoq_observe_asset` sets it unconditionally with the comment *"ASSUMPTION G3-3 (the kernel gates it to night)"*, and `twin.ottoq_sim_observe_asset` draws it at `night_walkaround_p` default **0.90**. So the hold cannot clear on completion; it clears on expiry, which is what `0260`'s measured **248-minute mean window** is. | `db/checks/0261` §1–2; the two-gate census, and the executor trace with `functions_naming_it = 3` against 10+ for every other service | **Fixed by 0383, and option (b) turned out not to exist.** The three candidates I offered assumed the walkaround held a staging stall; it never did, so "stop it holding a stall" had no referent. Option (a) was right for a reason I had not found: **no completer needed writing.** `twin.ottoq_sim_advance_visit_atoms` already completes ANY atom whose `ends_at` has passed, names no service and needs no bay — the gate is the START side, `ottoq_start_concurrent_atoms`, which admits `concurrency IN ('cabin','exterior','digital')`. 0383 derives the atom as **`exterior`** beside `sensor_clean` (event-raised, performed at the vehicle, `lane_stalls=NULL`, `at_perimeter:true` already declared on the atom), adds it to `ottoq_atom_retirable_set()` and declares it in `service_cadence_policy` — **one transaction, and never the retirable-set change first, or `ottoq_atoms_guard` stops demoting and 63 harmless atoms become permanent `must_do` blockers.** **`exterior` and not `digital` is the integrity of the change:** the starter meters `concurrency IN ('cabin','exterior')` against `general_tech` (**10** at the twin depot, one vehicle per tech), while `digital` is explicitly exempt from that pool — so the cheaper word would have had the twin complete 63 walkarounds with nobody walking, which is exactly the fabricated work completion I refused to write. It is deliberately NOT a probe of any starter's source — `bay`, `anchor` and `gate` are executed by three other seams, so a text probe would have reported three false orphans and made the real one look like a fourth. **What it does not catch, stated so nobody over-trusts it:** a class that completes without the work happening (the `credited`/`satisfied` shapes `ottoq_kpi_service_completion` separates). **Still open and now correctly separated from G86:** the 258 `twin.staging_overflow` events and the 82–98 long `perimeter_hold` parking claims are a staging-capacity-under-dwell question, and closing G86 frees not one stall. 'reopen'` (*"proves this visit was actually cut short"*), and **1 of the 30 ever had one.** Its doctrine is *"a vehicle resuming ITS OWN interrupted work"*; 29 had no work to resume, so the exit does not apply rather than failing. 'reopen'`, run-scoped | **TWO HYPOTHESES FORMED AND DISPROVED FIRST, both recorded because each looked solid.** **(a)** *"human-only exit in a twin with no humans"* — the declared machine really does permit `emergency_staged →` exit only to `command_center_operator`/`depot_supervisor` while entry is permitted to `ottoq_engine`, a genuine asymmetry, but not the cause. **(b)** *"the approval gate is permanently closed"* — `ottoq_ops_approvals` shows 1,792 declined / 203 expired / **0 approved**, which reads as a 0% grant rate. **It is not: 81 approvals were granted**, and every one carries `expired_from='approved'` with `expired_reason='run_ended'` because `ottoq_sim_release_depot` deliberately expires them (0097: *"no approval outlives its run… a surviving 'approved' row is readable by the NEXT run's gates"* — the 0046 divergence channel). The verdict was written only to `ottoq_sim_runs.validation_notes`, and `ottoq_purge_prior_runs` ends with `DELETE FROM ottoq_sim_runs`: **0 surviving cert-harness runs, 0 recoverable verdicts.** The comment that made it look handled — *"The verdict survives a dropped client: it lives on both run rows"* — is true of a dropped HTTP client and false of the next demo run. **Third instance after 0231 and 0340: a durability comment is not a durability mechanism.** The archive could not have held it either — `ottoq_archive_run` copies `v_run.payload`, and each arm is archived by `ottoq_sim_stop_and_reset` *inside the arm loop*, before the verdict exists. | `db/checks/0279` §1–2; **HTTP 524** at 2m05s even with `SET statement_timeout = 0`, so the `SET` lifts Postgres's limit and the edge proxy cuts it anyway). The `postgres` role carries no `statement_timeout`, which is why detached pg_cron execution works. **Recert now has an executor at all** — `ottoq_cert_columns` declared nine canons, `ottoq_cert_matrix` reported them, `ottoq_cert_recert_floor` expired them, and nothing ran them; the same declared-with-no-executor shape as G86. | **G84** *(**FIXED** by [0369](db/migrations/0369_a_vehicle_parked_in_one_stall_was_still_holding_another.sql) (applied `20260920041750`, `forces_recert TRUE`). The calendar and the reservation pointer disagree about the same stall. (The reactor's own comment always said the pointer is "the scarcer gate"; the size of the gap had never been counted.) Measured at 38 sim-minutes: **50 reserved-empty unexpired stalls, of which 36 were held by a vehicle sitting in a different stall** — and **21 of those 36 had no held/active booking** for that holder on that stall, against **15 that did**. The hierarchy it encodes: the calendar is the authority on intent, the pointer is a lock; a lock with no intent behind it is garbage, a lock with intent behind it is untouchable. **And one guard that is not optional:** `ottoq_stall_bookings.sim_run_id` is `NOT NULL`, so on a production call (`p_sim_run_id IS NULL`) the booking test can never match and the reclaimer would strip the entire site — the class is gated on `p_sim_run_id IS NOT NULL` and P9 refuses to ship without that gate. **Effect, measured one tick later:** staging reserved **101 → 88**, unclaimed staging **12 → 25**, total unclaimed stalls **~7 → 32**. | **fixed** — and what it leaves is a genuine capacity wall rather than a defect: **all 10 DCFC stalls claimed with 8 occupied, and 28 of 30 L2 claimed with 25 occupied.** That is the answer forming to "how many vehicles can this depot orchestrate", and it is a number, not a bug. |
| **G83** *(**FIXED** by [0368](db/migrations/0368_the_reroute_hunted_the_scarcest_stall_type_for_vehicles_that_wanted_the_most_plentiful.sql) (applied `20260920041236`, `forces_recert TRUE`). The second half of G82's answer, not a new subject: 0367 gave the reroute real stalls to offer, and this is what it did with them.)* | **The reroute searched for `dcfc` — ten stalls on this site — whenever the refused command carried no `stall_type`, including for vehicles whose actual target was a staging stall, of which there are 113.** `ottoq.ottoq_react_to_refusals` derived its search type as `COALESCE(payload->>'stall_type', CASE command_type WHEN 'stage' THEN 'staging' ELSE 'dcfc' END)`, so a `proceed_to_stall` without that key fell through to the scarcest resource on the depot. Measured on run `5b37ee46` (busy_day, seed 777777, twin depot, speed 8.0) at tick 38, with 0367 live from tick 1: of **31** refused commands, **14 `superseded`** and **10 `vehicle_state_incompatible`** are not reroutable and were correctly escalated; of the **7** reroutable, **2 rerouted** and **5 escalated `no_capacity`**. Split by what the walk looked for — **4 carried `stall_type='l2'` against an `l2` target** (2 of those rerouted), and **4 carried no `stall_type` at all against a STAGING target, for which the walk searched `dcfc`, found none, and escalated `no_capacity` while 41 stalls stood unclaimed.** The command already records where it was sent, in `payload->>'stall_id'`. | `db/checks/0258` §3 and Q3; the join from `ottoq_vehicle_commands.payload->>'stall_id'` to `stalls.stall_type` is the whole proof — payload type NULL, actual target `staging`, assumed `dcfc`, 4 rows, all escalated | **Fixed by 0368.** The wanted type is now read from `payload->>'stall_type'`, then from the stall the command was actually sent to, and only then from the command-type default; `stall_type_source` records which of the three answered. Part B is the observability half: `no_capacity` alone could not distinguish "the site is full" from "the walk looked in the wrong place", which is the ambiguity that hid this for a whole run, so escalations now carry `wanted_stall_type`, `stall_type_source` and `candidates_seen` in both the command payload and the `ottoq.refusal_escalated` event. P9 asserts every prior behaviour survived the patch — the `arrival_inspection` exclusion, the last-resort pass, `reroute_after`, `reacted_at`. | **fixed** — **and it carries a measurement mistake of mine that would have mispriced it:** the first reading of the reroute's success rate divided by ALL 31 refusals and reported **6.5%**, when 24 of those 31 are not reroutable by design. The honest figure is **2 of 7, 29%**. That is G78's defect — an honest refusal scored as a miss — committed by me on the instrument measuring the fix. `0258` Q3 now splits the classes and computes only the reroutable denominator. |
| **G82** *(**FIXED** by [0367](db/migrations/0367_the_reclaimer_0360_installed_has_never_run_on_the_live_metronome_path.sql) (applied `20260920041500`, `forces_recert TRUE`); after-measurement in [0258](db/checks/0258_the_reroute_loop_never_needed_building_it_needed_a_world_that_tells_it_the_truth.sql).)* | **The reservation reclaimer 0360 installed had never run once, because the live metronome does not call the function it was wired into — and that is why 296 of 346 refusals escalated to `no_capacity` while the reroute loop was working correctly.** `ottoq_demo_metronome` (cron job 12, the live simulation engine) calls `ottoq_sim_advance_tick_world` and `ottoq_sim_decide_and_dispatch` **directly**, at its own lines 100 and 108, and never calls `ottoq_sim_advance_tick` — which is where 0360 put `ottoq_release_unusable_reservations`. **G77's pattern reproduced inside the migration that was fixing G77:** the right routine, no live caller. Measured on run `3fb415d8` (cuOpt arm, seed 777777, busy_day, twin depot): **393 `ottoq.refusal_escalated` events over 1,245 ticks (0.316/tick), 296 of the first 346 `no_capacity` after `target_occupied`**; **38 release-eligible reservations standing and unchanged across a tick boundary** (tick 1123 → 1124); and all **4** DCFC stalls the reroute walk found calendar-free carried a reservation **36–45 sim-minutes past its own expiry**, **two of them holding no vehicle at all**. **And the architectural point, which is the opposite of what was expected:** the rejection → re-solve loop **already exists and already runs** — `ottoq.ottoq_react_to_refusals`, called from `decide_and_dispatch` line 121, walks up to 25 calendar-free stalls, reserves, books, and re-emits the command with `reroute_after`, escalating only when every candidate fails `ottoq_reserve_stall`. It had four legal moves and was refused all four by expired pointers. Nothing needed building; the loop needed a world that tells it the truth. **A second defect, found while proving the first, which would have hidden it again:** invoked directly the reclaimer returned `{"ok": false, "msg": "deadlock detected", "sqlstate": "40P01", "released": 0}`, and its call site is a bare `PERFORM`, so that JSON is discarded — **a reclaimer that releases nothing was indistinguishable from one with nothing to release.** **And the wiring check that missed all of this was fooled by a prefix:** `position('ottoq_sim_advance_tick' in prosrc)` returned 4179, which is the offset of `ottoq_sim_advance_tick_**world**`. Third instance of that mistake class here, after the `_`-is-a-LIKE-wildcard trap and the comment-vs-executable-SQL trap. A caller check must match the call syntax — `ottoq_sim_advance_tick(` — not the bare name. | `db/checks/0258` §1–3; the metronome's own source at lines 100/108; the 40P01 return quoted verbatim from a direct invocation | **Fixed by 0367.** Part A takes rows `FOR UPDATE ... SKIP LOCKED` so the reclaimer can never be a deadlock participant (contended rows are left for the next tick and counted in `skipped_locked`), counts `eligible` without locking, and emits `ottoq.reservation_reclaim_blocked` (registered in `ottoq_event_types_catalog`) when `eligible > 0 AND released = 0`. Part B was generated verbatim from `pg_get_functiondef` with one block inserted — not retyped, because 0360 Part C nearly broke every tick from a signature typed from memory. The call sits **above** the policy branch so every arm of an A/B gets the same world: reservation hygiene is part of the problem definition, not the policy (0146's argument about the L1 shield, applied to the calendar). **Immediate effect, measured 4 minutes after applying:** release-eligible reservations **38 → 1**, unclaimed stalls **11 → 41**, charge-type unclaimed **0 → 1**, `reservation_reclaim_blocked` **0**. | **fixed** — and it retracts an attribution, see the note on G76/G77 |

## Open, but diagnosed this session

| # | Finding | Evidence | Fix | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **G81** *(**MEASUREMENT AND WIRING BOTH FIXED**; consumption deliberately left. [0365](db/migrations/0365_the_overstay_has_a_name_and_no_reader_so_give_it_one.sql) (applied `20260920033500`) added `ottoq_stall_overstays(uuid)`, a run-scoped instrument — its first live read on `3fb415d8` found **8 concurrent overstays**, including **NASH-DCFC-STALL-09 held 188 minutes past its window** (one of only ten DCFC) and NASH-L2-STALL-31 at **241 minutes**. [0366](db/migrations/0366_the_frame_now_says_which_vehicles_are_overstaying_a_charge_stall.sql) (applied `20260920034500`) publishes `occupies_charge_stall_unbooked` on every frame vehicle, in the existing `g.facts >= 1` block beside `holds_charge_place`, reusing the `proposer_frame_facts` gate 0265/0287/0292 used. **Both `forces_recert FALSE`, and P4 asserts the grounds in each: no `ottoq_hash_*` atom reads the frame builder.** **Verified by two independent implementations agreeing:** the frame flags **7** vehicles and 0365s standalone instrument reports **7** charge-stall overstays on the same run, with all 116 vehicles carrying the key. A published fact that no separate instrument can confirm is how a key that is always false looks installed. **WHAT REMAINS, AND IT IS DELIBERATE: nothing consumes it yet.** Teaching `vehicle_is_held` to treat an overstaying vehicle as placed-in-fact is the change that actually stops the redundant offers, and it changes what the engine proposes — so it wants its own file and its own paired-seed before/after. Publishing and consuming were split on purpose after this entrys neighbour G80 needed three revisions for moving faster than its evidence.)* | **The engine detects an overstay, names it precisely, and publishes the name to nobody: `window_elapsed_occupied` has one writer and zero readers, 162 times on a single run.** This is the ROOT CAUSE under G80. `ottoq.ottoq_release_expired_bookings` releases a booking whose window has elapsed **while the vehicle is still occupying the stall**, and stamps `release_reason = 'window_elapsed_occupied'` — a deliberate, specific name for exactly the condition G80 chased. So the overstay is not unnoticed drift; it is a recognised state. **Measured on Arm A `3fb415d8`, and the ratio is the part that matters: `window_elapsed_occupied` = 162 against plain `window_elapsed` = 143.** More bookings expire with the asset still plugged in than expire cleanly — 53% of window expiries are overstays, so this is the normal case, not an edge case. **And nothing consumes it.** Searched exhaustively: the string occurs in **one** object in the whole database (the function that writes it) — no other function, no view, no matview — and nowhere in `edge-functions/`, `bridge/`, `proposer/` or `metrics/`. **A named signal with one writer and no reader.** The release itself is not wrong: it does not touch `stalls.current_vehicle_id`, so it never lies about physical occupancy — verified in G80, where 7 of 7 overstaying vehicles had the stall agreeing they were present. What is missing is any **reconciliation**: the calendar forgets the stall while the world still holds it, and every downstream reader (the decision frame, the proposer, the assignment path) then sees a free stall that is not free. G80 measures the price — **41** `assignment_refused_occupied` preflight refusals on this run, at least 8 naming a current overstayer. | the one-writer/zero-reader search across `pg_proc`, `pg_views`, `pg_matviews` and the repo's TypeScript and Python; the `release_reason` census on Arm A (162 / 143 / 22 / …); and the worked booking row for `b97789b5` on `NASH-L2-STALL-31` showing `state='done'`, `release_reason='window_elapsed_occupied'`, `source='reservation_honoured'`, window 06:57:26–07:14:32, `released_at` 07:14:44; **and one NEGATIVE check worth recording so it is not re-chased: the vehicle and stall records do NOT disagree with each other.** Of 57 vehicles claiming a stall on the twin depot, `stalls.current_vehicle_id` agrees with all **57**, with zero empty and zero naming a different vehicle. A conflict row appearing to show two claimants for one stall was two observations ~54 sim-minutes apart (conflict at sim 08:32, frame at 09:26), not a structural inconsistency — the same time-gap artifact that produced two of this register's corrections. So the divergence is **calendar versus world only**, which is exactly what makes `window_elapsed_occupied` the right signal to publish | **Not fixed here.** The cheapest honest fix is to make the signal readable rather than to change the release: publish the overstay so the frame carries it (a vehicle occupying a stall whose booking has been released), which lets `vehicle_is_held` and the proposer stop treating that stall as available, and lets the assignment path stop issuing commands preflight will refuse. `space_conflict_ledger` is the existing home for *"every calendar claim overruled by physical reality"* and already records the downstream refusals, so the overstay belongs beside them. **Do NOT fix it by re-extending the booking** — that would let a vehicle hold a stall indefinitely by not leaving, which is the thing the window exists to bound. | open — measured on live Arm A `3fb415d8`, twin depot only. Same family as G44 (rules declared with no caller) and `0349` (an attestation nothing checked): the system knows, says so, and no one is listening. It is the third time tonight that what looked like a solver problem turned out to be bookkeeping drift between the calendar and the physical world — after the reservation leak (G76) and G80. |
| **G80** | **Seven of twenty vehicles charging in a stall hold no live booking on it — an overstay — and the proposer, which correctly trusts the calendar, therefore offers them a different charge stall.** Found by the matched-frame comparison (`scripts/compare-plans-on-one-frame.sh`), which judges both solvers on the **identical** decision frame and so carries none of the run-level A/B's cadence confound. **The chain, every link measured on Arm A `3fb415d8`:** of **20** vehicles whose `current_state` is `charging_l2`/`charging_dcfc` with a `current_stall_id`, **13** have a covering live booking on that stall and **7 do not**. For all **7 of 7**, `stalls.current_vehicle_id = vehicle.id` — so the vehicle record and physical stall occupancy agree, and **the calendar is the outlier**. That is an overstay: the booking finished and the asset never left. `space_conflict_ledger` exists for exactly this (*"every calendar claim overruled by physical reality"*) and C7's failure library names `overstay` as one of the canonical nine. **Worked example.** `b97789b5` sits in `NASH-L2-STALL-31`, whose L2 booking is state **`done`** for the window 06:57:26–07:14:32 while the sim clock reads **09:26:53** — expired **2h12m** earlier. Its only *covering* booking is a **staging** stall, `NASH-STG-S031` (07:40–12:00). So the frame's `holds_charge_booking = false`, `holds_charge_reservation = false`, `holds_charge_place = false`, `has_live_booking = true` are **all correct**. `vehicle_is_held` short-circuits on `holds_charge_place` — its docstring says that answer *"wins outright"* — so `has_live_booking` is never consulted, the vehicle is judged plannable, and CP-SAT offers it `NASH-L2-STALL-19` at 19 kW against `soc 70 / target_soc 100`. **Given the calendar, that offer is defensible.** Given the physical world it is wasteful, because the vehicle is already drawing power in a charge stall. **AMENDMENT — the cost is measured, and the engine is SAFE.** `space_conflict_ledger` holds **41** rows for this run, every one `conflict_kind='assignment_refused_occupied'` with `resolution='command_refused_preflight'`: a `begin_charge` command stopped because another vehicle was physically present, `detail.code='target_occupied'`. Of those 41, **8** name a *currently*-overstaying vehicle as the present occupant — a **lower bound**, since the overstay set is a snapshot at one clock and the other 33 involve stalls whose occupant has since moved. So the chain completes: a booking reaches `done` while the asset stays plugged in → the calendar shows the stall free while it is physically occupied → an assignment is made to it → preflight refuses the command. **Nothing is double-booked and nothing unsafe happens** — this is precisely the assignment-plus-verification pair CLAUDE.md rule 6 says never to remove, doing its job 41 times. **The cost is wasted work, not risk:** 41 refused commands, plus proposer effort spent offering charge stalls to vehicles already drawing power. **CORRECTION — IT DOES NOT ACCUMULATE, AND I CLAIMED IT DID FROM TWO POINTS.** This amendment first read *"it accumulates, which is what makes it a leak rather than a regime… 7 of 20 (35%) to 10 of 18 (56%)"* and called it the same signature as the reservation leak `0360` fixed. Sampled properly across Arm A the share **fluctuates and if anything declines**: tick 455 → 10 of 18 (**55%**), tick 489 → 6 of 15 (40%), tick 521 → 5 of 22 (**22%**), tick 556 → 7 of 23 (30%). **What is true is persistence, not growth:** an overstay is present at every sample, at **22–55%** of vehicles charging in a stall. That is a standing regime, and a regime at that level is worth fixing on its own terms — but it is not a leak and the reservation-leak analogy is withdrawn. The rising `conflicts_logged` (41 → 45 → 58 → 66 across those same ticks) is **not** evidence of worsening either: `space_conflict_ledger` is cumulative, so it can only rise. Its *rate* is roughly steady at ~0.25 refusals per tick. **Instant-consistent pool figure**, measured from the frame rather than across two moments: of 116 frame vehicles the serviceable pool is **25, all 25 already in a stall**, and the frame holds zero `staged_awaiting_service` and zero `arrived_at_gate`. | the matched-frame log (`matched_3fb415d8.jsonl`, contamination asserted 0 on every sample, so the observation was passive); the 20 / 13 / 7 booking-coverage census; the 7-of-7 physical-agreement check that identifies the calendar as the outlier; this vehicle's booking rows read directly with stall codes, types, states and windows; and the frame's four hold-flags read against `vehicle_is_held`'s actual short-circuit order | **Not fixed here, and the fix is NOT the one this finding first proposed.** The real defect is the overstay: a booking reaching `done` while its vehicle still occupies the stall, with nothing reconciling the two. Fixing the proposer instead would be treating the symptom. When it is addressed, the honest options are to release-and-rebook the overstaying vehicle so the calendar matches the world, or to record the overstay in `space_conflict_ledger` and let the proposer see it. **A move-cost term in the objective is still worth having** so a lateral move can never look free, but it is a mitigation, not the cause. | open — measured on live Arm A `3fb415d8`, twin depot only. **THIS ENTRY HAS BEEN WRONG TWICE AND BOTH ARE RECORDED RATHER THAN OVERWRITTEN.** (1) It first claimed *31 of 50 (62%)* of the pool was already stalled; that census came from `vehicles` ~85 sim-minutes from the frame that produced the offer, the clock-domain error class of G75 and `0251` §5. (2) It then claimed the offer was a **gratuitous lateral move to a mid-charge vehicle with no declared need**. The vehicle is physically mid-charge, but it holds **no booked charge place** and is **30 points below target**, so the offer is reasonable on the evidence the proposer is given — and the `svc_step: None` I cited is not a statement that nothing is needed. (3) Consequently the recommendation I drew from it — *narrow the pool, exclude the charging states* — **would be wrong**: it would discard exactly these 7 genuine cases. The `--states` test that returned `serviceable = 0` proves only that this frame held no gate arrivals at that instant, not that CP-SAT had no real work. |
| **G79** *(**FIXED** by [0362](db/migrations/0362_a_worse_ranked_proposer_removes_a_better_one_from_the_pool_before_rank_is_read.sql) (applied `20260920024530`) + [0363](db/migrations/0363_the_gate_0362_installed_could_never_be_opened.sql) (applied `20260920025230`), both `forces_recert FALSE`, gated by `proposer_rank_protects_supersede`. **Measured with the gate ON on run `f14d5620`, fire_id 118 / tick 598: `forward_lex` rows lost to `newer_proposal_same_entity` went to ZERO**, while cuOpt's 16 self-supersessions continued — equal ranks still supersede on recency, which is the intended behaviour. **AND THE REMAINING 9 SUPERSESSIONS TURNED OUT TO BE CORRECT, which is worth more than the fix:** they carry `entity_decided_by_other_proposal`, written by `ottoq_decide_tick`, which also does not read precedence — so they looked like a second instance of the same defect. They are not: **9 of 9 of those vehicles are physically in a stall.** They were served, so the `forward_lex` proposal for them was genuinely moot. `ottoq_decide_tick` needs no rank guard. **No starvation, which was the whole reason for the gate:** the failure mode feared was a protected proposal holding a vehicle forever, and every affected vehicle was placed. That measurement is what would justify promoting the gate from measured to enforced. One residual naming imprecision, not filed as its own finding because nothing is lost by it: 0 of those 9 entities had an *enacted proposal*, so `entity_decided_by_other_proposal` names a proposal where the local decide path did the placing — G78's family, cosmetic here.)* | **Proposer rank IS enforced where pending proposals are SELECTED and was not consulted where they are SUPERSEDED — so a later-submitting worse-ranked proposer removed a better-ranked proposal from the pool before the rank-ordered selection could prefer it.** `ottoq_l2_external_proposal` sorts on declared data first: `ORDER BY COALESCE((SELECT pp.rank FROM ottoq_proposer_precedence pp WHERE pp.source = p.source), 2147483647) ASC, p.created_at DESC, …` — rank first, unlisted at the maximum, recency only as a tie-break. **That half always worked.** But `ottoq_submit_external_proposal` superseded unconditionally — no rank term, no source term, and the string `ottoq_proposer_precedence` did not occur in it — and supersession moves rows out of `pending`, the only status that `ORDER BY` can see. So cuOpt (**rank 10**, *"service-failure fallback. Lower priority than CP-SAT"*), firing every 30-second tick, removed `forward_lex` (**rank 0**, the declared primary CP-SAT proposer) from the candidate pool before ranking happened. **A pool filtered by recency before a correct ranking is applied.** Measured before the fix on run `f14d5620`: of 26 superseded `forward_lex` rows, 12 had a later `cuopt` proposal for the same entity; the other 14 were not claimed as cuOpt's doing. | the two `ORDER BY` / `UPDATE` bodies read directly; `position()` returning 0 for `ottoq_proposer_precedence` in both supersede writers; the 12-of-26 entity-matched count before; and after the fix, zero `newer_proposal_same_entity` on `forward_lex` with 9 of 9 remaining supersessions resolving to vehicles physically in stalls | **Fixed, and the fix reuses the ordering that already works (rule 5):** the guard does not rank anything, it declines to DELETE a strictly better-ranked candidate and lets the existing sort judge it. Strictly better only, so a proposer refreshing its own plan still replaces it — a stale plan outliving its world is G62/G71. **Remaining work is promotion, not repair:** the gate is set on one run; enabling it by default wants a second run's worth of the same no-starvation measurement, and per 0362's own note **the run where the key is first set to 1 is the run that needs a fresh canon.** | **fixed — measured-not-enforced.** Also note the defect *inside* the fix: 0362 shipped a gate that could not be opened, because `ottoq_policy_params.param_key` has an FK to `ottoq_policy_param_catalog` and the key was unregistered. Caught within minutes by trying to use it (23503); 0363 registers it. Credit to the FK — it refused the write rather than letting a permanently-closed gate read as installed. |
| **G78** | **The disposer has no concept of an abstention, so a proposer that correctly declines is recorded as a refusal for a malformed stall id — and the more honest the proposer, the worse it scores.** Measured on the first `forward_lex` fire this engine has ever had (fire_id 116, tick 442, run `f14d5620`): of 16 submitted rows, **11 were deliberate abstentions** carrying `abstain: true`, no `stall_id`, and an explicit rationale — *"planned to start at +111 min on l2 95fe1b50…, beyond this tick's 30-min window; re-offered when due"*. That is CP-SAT planning a horizon and offering only what is due now, which is the correct behaviour for a rolling re-solve. The disposer scored one of them **`refused` / `invalid_stall_id`** and superseded the rest. Established rather than inferred: `ottoq_dispose_external_proposals` contains `invalid_stall_id` at char 1,958 and **does not contain the string `'abstain'` anywhere (position 0)** — it has no abstention branch at all, so a row with `abstain: true` falls through to the malformed-stall-id path. **Why it is worth filing rather than shrugging at:** nothing unsafe happens — an abstention is correctly not enacted — but the refusal tally is the instrument by which proposer quality gets judged, and it now charges a proposer for declining. cuOpt largely does not abstain, so it never paid this; CP-SAT abstains **11 of 16 rows by design**, so it pays it constantly. Any A/B that scores refusals would conclude the more careful solver is the worse one. **Same defect class as G74 (a rule reporting a pass for a check it never ran) and G73 (a verdict whose only possible word was the wrong one): an instrument mislabelling correct behaviour, which is the failure mode most likely to be believed.** | the disposition census on run `f14d5620` after fire_id 116, the refused row inspected directly (`abstain = "true"`, `stall_id` NULL, `verb = assign_stall`), and the comment-stripped `position()` scan of `ottoq_dispose_external_proposals` showing `invalid_stall_id` present and `'abstain'` absent | **Not fixed here.** Give the disposer an abstention branch before the stall-id validation: a row with `abstain: true` is neither enacted nor refused — it is a recorded declination, ideally its own `disposition_reason` (`proposer_abstained`) so the refusal tally stops absorbing it. Cheap, and it must land before any A/B scores refusals across proposers, or the comparison is rigged against the better solver. | open — measured on live run `f14d5620`, twin depot only. Found by firing CP-SAT for the first time, which is the general point: the abstention path had never been exercised because the only proposer that had ever run barely abstains. |
| **G76** *(**AND THE FIX CREDITED HERE WAS MIS-ATTRIBUTED — corrected by G82.** The improvement recorded when 0360 applied — free stalls 2 → 30, charge-type 0 → 11, occupancy 21 → 43 — was real but **did not come from 0360's tick wiring**, which G82 proves had never executed on a metronome-driven run. It came from the one-off direct invocation of `ottoq_release_unusable_reservations` made while verifying that migration. A single manual call is not a fix; it looked like one because the numbers moved. 0367 is what actually puts the reclaimer on the live path.)* | **The twin depot is 98% reserved and 13% occupied: the binding constraint is reservation hygiene, not capacity, and not solver quality.** Measured on live run `f14d5620` (busy_day, twin depot, rule 8), depot-wide: **155 of 158 stalls carry a reservation, 21 hold a vehicle, and 135 are reserved-and-empty.** Only **2 of 158** stalls are unclaimed by anything — so a proposer's legal move set is ~1% of the site while 87% of the site is physically free. The sharpest slice is the one Chase named as the pressure valve: **113 staging stalls, all 113 reserved, 4 occupied, 109 reserved-and-empty** — and 29 of those staging reservations are ALREADY EXPIRED against the sim clock and not reclaimed. On the 40 charge stalls the picture is the same (40/40 reserved, 0 truly free) and **8 of them, all L2, are held by vehicles that cannot drive to them — 6 `tow_requested`, 2 `staged_for_departure`**; **zero routines in the database mention both `tow_requested` and `reserved_by`**, so nothing releases a reservation when a vehicle is immobilised. **This is the upstream cause of three symptoms that were being read as solver problems.** The CP-SAT bridge abstains with *"40 charge-capable stall(s) and not one is offerable this tick"* — correctly. cuOpt's proposals land as 4 enacted / 7 refused / 60 superseded because it is proposing into a fully pre-committed site. And Nemotron's objective directive (`readiness_first` / `throughput_first` / `energy_balanced`) is moot when no solver has a legal move. **No optimiser can outperform another when neither has an admissible action.** **Confirmed across ticks, not one instant:** sampled at ticks 337 and 344, `truly_free` = 0 of 40 and CP-SAT `status='empty'` both times, dead-weight holders 7 then 9. | the CP-SAT fire record via the offline bridge route (`bridge/proposer_bridge --frame/--classes/--emit-sql`, which needs no DSN, no edge secret and no host), plus the depot-scoped stall census and the holder-state census joined through `stalls.reserved_by` | **Not fixed here, and the obvious fix is wrong — see G77.** Two separable pieces: (a) release a stall reservation when its holder enters a state that cannot consume it (`tow_requested`, `staged_for_departure`), which is the one-function change and returns ~20% of charge capacity; (b) reclaim reservations that have expired on the SIM clock, which is 29 staging stalls on this run. **Do NOT implement (b) by scheduling `ottoq_gc_stale_reservations` — G77 is why.** | open — measured on live run `f14d5620`, twin depot only. **Directly on Chase's stated goal** (how many vehicles this depot can stage, sort and orchestrate at once): the answer today is limited by reservations that outlive their purpose, not by stalls. |

| **G69** 🔴 | **A credential this repo removed twelve days ago is still live in production, in two places, and the finding that named it was never closed.** On **2026-09-07** `edge-functions/ottoq-energy-mpc/index.ts` had three hardcoded fallbacks removed and a fail-closed guard added, with its own comment saying the shared secret "is in history and must be treated as compromised regardless of what this file says now." **The fix was never deployed.** Deployed **v4, dated 2026-07-15**, still reads `Deno.env.get("OTTOQ_INTEL_URL") ?? "http://<internal host>:8080"`, `OTTOQ_INTEL_TOKEN ?? "<27-char secret>"`, `OTTOQ_BRIDGE_TOKEN ?? "<the same secret>"`, and has **no** `if (!BRIDGE_TOKEN …) return 500` guard. **And it is not only the edge function:** three live overloads of `public.ottoq_energy_mpc_replan` carry that same token as a **`DEFAULT` argument value**, dumped into the tracked `db/baseline/functions_public.sql`. **This is not a new finding** — `docs/MAGENTA_AUDIT.md` §198–228 named the secret, the plain-HTTP host, the dual use and the rotation step. What is new is the measurement that it is **still in force**, which nothing had checked, and which every drift signal this repo used reported as "unchanged". | the deployed body, pulled by `supabase functions download --use-api` and diffed against the repo (18 lines, 1,192 bytes); `pg_get_function_arguments` over `pg_proc` (3 of 3 `ottoq_energy_mpc_replan` overloads carry the token as a default); `has_function_privilege` (**`anon` cannot execute, `authenticated` can**); `ottoq_policy_params` for `energy_mpc_follow` (set once, 2026-07-15, one run scope, long purged) | **Not fixed here, and not fixable by me alone.** Two founder actions, in this order: **(1) rotate** — the 27-character secret is burned because it is in git history; set a new `OTTOQ_BRIDGE_TOKEN` **and** update the twin's `pg_net` caller in the same change, or the bridge starts refusing its own caller, and drop the `DEFAULT` from all three `ottoq_energy_mpc_replan` overloads. **(2) deploy the repo's version**, which fails closed: with the intel variables unset it returns `500 bridge not configured` instead of proxying to a hardcoded host. **The repo copy is left untouched on purpose** — syncing the deployed body in would re-commit the credential and undo the 09-07 fix, which is why `scripts/check-edge-drift.sh` lists this function as an acknowledged exception with the reason written beside it rather than muting it. | open — **founder action.** Stated at its real size: the edge function is a **fixed-target proxy**.
