It does **not** fix the shape. | open — the index shipped; bounding the start-path purge has not. A start whose cost grows with all history is a landmine that regrows. Bound it against the measured cost of the first successful purge, not a guess. |
| **G66** | **The declared rank-0 proposer does not fire at all on a fully armed run, which is a different defect from G60 and must not be filed as its recurrence.** Run `dde654cc` (2026-09-19 17:09, `ottoq_agentic_arming` verdict **armed, 7 of 7**, `prearrival_charge_yields_to_solver=1`) ticked past 40 ticks with cuOpt calling NVIDIA live and enacting 21 `stall_assignment` rows — and `ottoq_proposer_fire_log` recorded **zero fires during the run**. Its whole content is `forward_lex`: 75 empty fires (last 2026-09-17 00:29:33) and 37 submitted fires / 208 proposals (last 2026-09-14 09:23:34), both predating the run. So `ottoq_agent_review`'s `empty_fires: 0` is not "fired and found nothing", it is **never fired**, and the verdict is `solver_returned_nothing` rather than G60's `solver_saw_empty_frame`. | `ottoq_proposer_fire_log` grouped by (declared_source, status) with `max(fired_at)` read against the run's `started_at`; `ottoq_agent_review(run,3)`; `ottoq_activity_feed(run,400)` | **unfixed.** 0339 opened the gate that was taking the scarce resource before the fire; the fire itself is not happening, one stage earlier. A fix aimed at G60's mechanism cannot move this, and the A/B that proved G60 says nothing about it. | open — this is PR #194's outstanding item, now localised: `fires_with_headroom` cannot become non-zero while `fires` is zero. Evidence: [0248](db/checks/0248_the_twin_start_door_opens_and_the_whole_loop_is_visible_on_a_live_armed_run.sql) |
| **G68** | **CP-SAT is absent, not broken, and the fallback hid it so well that the attestation reported "armed".** `ottoq-cpsat-propose` v5 requires `OTTOQ_INTEL_URL` and `OTTOQ_INTEL_TOKEN`; both unset, so it throws `"CP-SAT service is not configured"` before building a request, and its catch returns **HTTP 200** with the cuOpt fallback. `ottoq_proposer_submit_batch(p_source:='forward_lex')` — the only writer of `ottoq_proposer_fire_log` — is therefore never reached, which is the mechanism behind G66. Measured on run `dde654cc`: **60 of 60** agent chains, ticks 1→221, identical reason. `cpsat_service`'s last ledger call is 2026-09-14 09:23:39. | `ottoq_decisions.enacted_action->'solver_handoff'` grouped over the run; deployed bodies of both edge functions; `ottoq_intelligence_ledger` | [0349](db/migrations/0349_a_run_reads_armed_while_its_rank_zero_proposer_is_unreachable.sql) makes it **visible** — `ottoq_agentic_arming` gains a `primary_proposer` block and a new verdict `armed_primary_unreachable`, proven both ways (A2 requires the verdict on the live defect; A3 plants one fire row in a rolled-back subtransaction and requires `reachable` to return to `true`, so the signal is a measurement and not a constant). | open — **founder action, not mine.** The two secrets must be set and the `ottoq-intelligence` host (FastAPI + `ortools==9.15.6755`) must be running. The bridge's 5-second `AbortSignal.timeout` and its comment that "the solver host may be intentionally stopped between development sessions" are correct and were left alone. |
| **G67** | **The repo is stale on the six edge functions that own the loop, and nothing detects it.** Traced while diagnosing G66: the repo's `ottoq-orchestrator-agent/index.ts` ends its handoff with `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil(fetch(...))` and can emit only `queued`/`disabled`/`gate_error`; the **deployed v26** awaits the bridge and emits `completed`/`fallback`/`failed` with `engine` and `fallback_reason`. Every live agent decision records `{status: fallback, engine: cuopt, fallback_reason: "CP-SAT service is not configured"}` — **a shape the repo's copy cannot produce.** Six functions changed since the 2026-08-03 manifest snapshot and **every one is on the agent/solver/twin loop**: `ottoq-cpsat-propose`, `ottoq-orchestrator-agent`, `otto-twin-control`, `ottoq-cuopt-propose`, `ottoq-orchestrate-tick`, `ottoq-assign-optimize`. Anyone auditing the loop from the repo reads code that is not running. **And the old manifest's drift signal was worse than none:** its `version` column is not the API's — `ottoq-wave-admit` reads v2 there and v5 live with an identical `updated_at`, so diffing it flags ~26 of 28 functions when 6 changed, a 77% false-positive rate. | `list_edge_functions` `updated_at`/`sha256` against the 2026-08-03 snapshot; deployed bodies of `ottoq-orchestrator-agent` v26 and `ottoq-cpsat-propose` v5 read in full and compared to the on-disk copies | `edge-functions/_MANIFEST.md` refreshed 2026-09-19: the 6 stale copies named with their deployed `sha256`, the withdrawn version-column claim, and `updated_at` adopted as the signal. **The six were deliberately NOT hand-synced** — transcribing ~400 lines of deployed TypeScript out of an API response risks a silent one-character divergence, and a copy that looks synced is worse than one labelled stale. They need a real Management-API pull verified against the recorded hashes. **Then done properly, 2026-09-19:** with a Supabase access token the founder supplied, all 28 ACTIVE functions were pulled with `supabase functions download --use-api` (no Docker, no transcription — the CLI unbundles server-side and writes source straight to disk) and compared by SHA-256. Four were synced into the repo; `scripts/check-edge-drift.sh` makes the comparison repeatable, refuses to report a pass when it could not compare (no token/CLI → **exit 2**), and is mutation-proven in both directions (a one-line change to a repo copy reports `DRIFT` and exits 1). | **CLOSED — and the pull corrected the drift list it was based on.** Measured by content hash, **five** functions differed, not six: `ottoq-cuopt-propose` and `ottoq-assign-optimize` were flagged by `updated_at` and are **byte-identical** (false positives), and **`ottoq-energy-mpc` was NOT flagged and differs** (false negative — its `updated_at` of 2026-07-15 predates the snapshot). So **both** drift signals this repo has used were wrong: `version` at a 77% false-positive rate, and `updated_at` at 2 false positives + 1 false negative out of 28. The false negative is the one whose drift contains a live credential — see **G69**. Repo is now **27 of 28** in sync, the 28th deliberately. |

## Closed

| # | Finding | Closed by |
|---|---|---|
| G2 | Anon `EXECUTE` on privileged mutations; proposals and overrides took identity from the client. | server-derived identity |
| G3 | Two need-derivation paths — twin and real feed — that could disagree. | one shared path reading asset state |
| G4 | The verdict could not see the proposal stream. | `h_prop` + `h_defr` |
| G5 | The L1 rule-evaluation ledger was not run-scoped. | run-scoping migration |
| G6 | The recall decision left no ledger row. | [0206](db/migrations/0206_every_recall_decision_is_a_ledger_row_and_the_implementation_is_a_name.sql) |
| G7 | The work side had no way to refuse a recall. | [0211](db/migrations/0211_the_work_side_can_refuse_a_recall_and_the_refusal_is_a_ledger_row.sql), [0212](db/migrations/0212_the_tick_asks_the_work_side_before_it_books_the_stall.sql) |
| G11 | The event stream carried no sim time. | sim-clock column on `ottoq_events` |
| G15 | The L1 shield read the wall clock inside the twin — 12 of 29 active evaluators. | [0204](db/migrations/0204_the_run_has_one_clock_and_the_shield_and_the_events_read_it.sql) |
| G18 | The repo had stopped following the change-control discipline `scripts/APPLYING.md` documents: 93 migration files carried no version header, 98 carried invented ones, and CI never looked at `db/migrations` at all. | `tests/test_migration_hygiene.py`, `scripts/gen-migration-index.py`, `db/migrations/UNFILED.md` |
| G17 | KPI-4's touch vocabulary was seven-eighths unsatisfiable — seven of its eight actor types were rejected by the `ottoq_events` CHECK — and it missed every real human actor the constraint does permit. | [0213](db/migrations/0213_kpi_four_counted_seven_actor_types_that_cannot_exist.sql) — vocabulary is now a table pinned to the live CHECK; 20 classified, 8 human, none uninsertable |
| G19 | The certification pair got ~2x slower in seven days on a fixed workload. `ottoq_boot_state_fingerprint` — the enforced `endst` atom — serialized and MD5'd **1,360,915 rows per call**, four calls per pair, to characterise **13**. | [0222](db/migrations/0222_the_fingerprint_hashed_a_million_rows_to_report_thirteen.sql) — the fingerprint measured **260.6 ms / 218.2 ms** against ~64,000 ms after; round 26 a and b landed **537 s and 533 s** against a 16-pair baseline of min 643 / mean 755. Evidence: [0129](db/checks/0129_the_fingerprint_hashes_five_million_rows_to_report_thirteen.sql), [0126](db/checks/0126_where_the_tick_actually_spends_its_time.sql), [0128](db/checks/0128_the_pair_reads_nine_gigabytes_and_seven_of_them_are_five_scans_of_one_table.sql) (conclusion superseded) |
| G20 | The second SDR emitter would bill a DCFC charge at the L2 tariff: `charge` is the one `svc_code` mapping to two operations and the lookup picked with an unordered `LIMIT 1`. Dormant since 2026-06-18, so nothing burned. | [0220](db/migrations/0220_the_schedule_task_emitter_asks_the_stall_what_kind_of_charge_it_was.sql) — the trigger asks `stalls.stall_type`, not the heap. Evidence: [0124](db/checks/0124_the_other_sdr_emitter_would_bill_a_dcfc_charge_at_the_l2_tariff.sql) |
| G63 | **The Twin start door had never opened: `ottoq_start_demo_run` could not complete in this engine's recorded life.** Four defects in sequence, each hidden behind the one before it. | [0344](db/migrations/0344_the_purge_cannot_complete_and_the_twin_start_door_has_no_handler.sql) (registry check (b) narrowed to run-key columns, blocking FK dropped) → [0345](db/migrations/0345_the_purge_deletes_engine_tables_alphabetically_not_by_dependency.sql) (step (4) ordered by real FK depth, not alphabetically) → [0347](db/migrations/0347_one_missing_index_made_the_twin_start_door_cost_six_thousand_gigabytes.sql) (unindexed FK child column made the purge quadratic: 30.447 ms → 0.159 ms per probe) → [0348](db/migrations/0348_a_six_row_table_held_the_twin_start_door_shut_because_its_guard_never_learned_the_purge_protocol.sql) (a **six-row** table whose append-only guard never learned the purge's arming protocol, which also made `ottoq_recall_decisions`' 283k rows undeletable via FK). **Measured end to end:** run `dde654cc`, 7,032,541 rows purged in 210,880 ms, `retry_passes: 0`, armed 7 of 7, ticking. Evidence: [0248](db/checks/0248_the_twin_start_door_opens_and_the_whole_loop_is_visible_on_a_live_armed_run.sql) |
| G60 | **The rank-0 primary proposer was starved by a contract signed forty minutes before the vehicle arrived.** 0335 made CP-SAT (`forward_lex`) the primary assignment proposer; it submitted **nothing** across 75 fires, and in 74 of them `n_in_serviceable_state` equalled `n_vehicles_held` exactly. `ottoq_sim_prearrival_contracts` reserves a dcfc/l2 stall for every `en_route_to_depot` vehicle from the WORLD beat, so by `arrived_at_gate` — the only state the fire and `ottoq_decide_tick` act on — the choice was already made. 0337 could not fix it: a reservation taken forty minutes ahead is not a race. | [0339](db/migrations/0339_the_charge_stall_was_reserved_before_any_solver_could_see_it.sql) — run-scoped `prearrival_charge_yields_to_solver`, default 0 = unchanged, granted by `ottoq_agentic_arm`; attestation went six keys to seven. **Proven by controlled A/B on the live function, both arms in one aborted transaction: 23 en-route vehicles, dial=0 took 23 of 23 charge stalls, dial=1 took 0 and granted 23 staging.** Evidence: [0247](db/checks/0247_the_primary_solver_was_starved_by_a_contract_written_forty_minutes_early.sql) |

## Measured leads — not yet adversarially refuted

Stated separately because the discipline of this page is that a finding names
the guard that closes it. These are measured but the diagnosis is not finished,
and each says exactly what would falsify it.

| # | Lead | What is measured, and what is not |
|---|---|---|
| G61 | **The run-scope registry is per-column; its FK guard is per-table, so a mixed-class table is required to block the purge it is registered to survive.** `ottoq_proposer_fire_log` has two rows — `sim_run_id` class `evidence` and `tick_seq` class `stamp` — and `ottoq_check_run_scope_registry` check (b) asks "does this TABLE have an FK to `ottoq_sim_runs`". So the stamp row demands the FK that defeats the evidence row. | **Measured:** dropping that FK made the guard report a blocking defect, which refused migration 0340 outright (assertion A3b). 112 fire-log rows name runs in the purge's doomed set. **Not established:** whether the right fix is narrowing check (b) to the registered column, reclassifying `tick_seq`, or giving evidence tables a nullable FK. 0340 sidesteps it by registering exactly one column and pins that with A3d. |

These are open, they are the highest-severity items on this page, and no
migration can close them.

| # | Finding | State |
|---|---|---|
| **S-01 / S-02** | A shared secret was committed to the repository. The **code half is fixed**: the literal is gone and the bridge fails closed rather than open, pinned by `ottoq-intelligence/tests/test_auth_fails_closed.py`. A committed secret stays compromised until it is **rotated**, which is an operator action on EC2 and in Supabase. | open — awaiting rotation |

Everything else in [`docs/MAGENTA_AUDIT.md`](docs/MAGENTA_AUDIT.md) — 85 of 87
findings — is closed, each `FIXED` naming the guard that fails without it.

## The other thing on this page that is not ours to close

`db/migrations/UNFILED.md` lists **67 migrations, 734,007 characters**, applied to the
database with no committed file. They are recoverable from `ottoq_schema_snapshots`
and the function catalog. Whether to spend the time recovering them is Chase's
call, not the build track's, and the drift check reports **INVESTIGATE** rather
than CLEAN until it is made.
